## Not I / 非我也

===Original===
Some like drink
In a pint pot,
Some like to think;
Some not.

Strong Dutch Cheese,
Old Kentucky Rye,
Some like these;
Not I.

Some like Poe
And others like Scott,
Some like Mrs. Stowe;
Some not.

Some like to laugh,
Some like to cry.
Some like chaff;
Not I.
===Chinese===
有人爱喝酒，
品脱大杯装，
有人爱琢磨；
有人却不尝。

醇厚的荷兰奶酪，
陈年肯塔基黑麦酒，
有人就好这一口；
非我也。

有人爱读坡，
也有爱读司各特，
有人爱斯托夫人；
有人却不。

有人爱欢笑，
有人爱哭泣。
有人爱打趣；
非我也。

## Here, Perfect to a Wish / 尽如人意

===Original===
Here, perfect to a wish,
We offer, not a dish,
But just the platter:
A book that's not a book,
A pamphlet in the look
But not the matter.

I own in disarray;
As to the flowers of May
The frosts of Winter,
To my poetic rage,
The smallness of the page
And of the printer.
===Chinese===
此处，尽如人意，
我们奉上的，不是菜肴，
不过是个盘子：
是一本算不上书的书，
外表像本小册子
内容却算不上。

我承认，杂乱无章；
正如五月的鲜花
遇上冬日的寒霜，
之于我诗情澎湃，
版面如此之小，
印刷如此简陋。

## As Seamen on the Seas / 宛若水手航行海上

===Original===
As seamen on the seas
With song and dance descry
Adown the morning breeze
An islet in the sky:
In Araby the dry,
As o'er the sandy plain
The panting camels cry
To smell the coming rain.

So all things over earth
A common law obey
And rarity and worth
Pass, arm in arm, away;
And even so, today,
The printer and the bard,
In pressless Davos, pray
Their sixpenny reward.
===Chinese===
宛如水手航行海上，
又歌又舞地望见
乘着清晨的微风
天际一座小岛浮现：
又如干旱的阿拉伯，
跋涉在漫漫黄沙间，
喘息的骆驼齐声叫唤，
闻到了将至的雨水甘甜。

世间万物如此，
都遵循同一条法则：
稀有与可贵，
总是携手同行、一同走过；
即便今日也是如此，
印刷匠与诗人携手，
在没有印刷机的达沃斯，
祈求那六便士的酬报。

## The Pamphlet Here Presented / 此处呈献之小册

===Original===
The pamphlet here presented
Was planned and printed by
A printer unindent-ed,
A bard whom all decry.

The author and the printer,
With various kinds of skill,
Concocted it in Winter
At Davos on the Hill.

They burned the nightly taper
But now the work is ripe
Observe the costly paper,
Remark the perfect type!
===Chinese===
此处所呈之小册，
策划与印制出自
一名未经学徒期的印刷匠，
与一位为人非议的诗人。

作者与印刷匠，
各怀不同的本事，
在冬日里共同炮制
于山上的达沃斯。

他们夜夜燃尽烛芯，
如今大功总算告成，
请看这昂贵的纸张，
请看这完美的铅字！

## See How the Children in the Print / 看画中孩童

===Original===
*
See how the children in the print
Bound on the book to see what's in't!
O, like these pretty babes, may you
Seize and apply this volume too!
And while your eye upon the cuts
With harmless ardour open and shuts,
Reader, may your immortal mind
To their sage lessons not be blind.
===Chinese===
*
看那画中的孩童，
扑到书前要看其中内容！
啊，愿你如同这些可爱的娃娃，
也把这一卷牢牢抓下！
当你的双眼在版画上
带着无害的热忱一开一阖，
读者啊，愿你那不死的灵魂，
莫对他们睿智的教诲视而不见。

## Reader, Your Soul Upraise to See / 读者，且高扬你的心灵来看

===Original===
Reader, your soul upraise to see,
In yon fair cut designed by me,
The pauper by the highwayside
Vainly soliciting from pride.

Mark how the Beau with easy air
Contemns the anxious rustic's prayer,
And casting a disdainful eye,
Goes gaily gallivanting by.

He from the poor averts his head.…
He will regret it when he's dead.
===Chinese===
读者啊，且高扬心灵来看，
在我所绘的那幅精美版画中，
路旁的乞丐苦苦哀怜，
却徒然向傲慢者求告。

看那花花公子何等从容，
轻蔑了乡下人焦急的央求，
他投去一道鄙夷的目光，
便欢快地招摇而过。

他把头扭开，不看穷苦人……
待到死后，他必将追悔莫及。

## A Peak in Darien / 达连之巅

===Original===
Broad gazing on untrodden lands,
See where adventurous Cortez stands;
While in the heavens above his head,
The Eagle seeks its daily bread.

How aptly fact to fact replies:
Heroes and Eagles, hills and skies.

Ye, who contemn the fatted slave,
Look on this emblem and be brave
===Chinese===
极目远眺无人涉足的疆土，
看那英勇的科尔特斯伫立何处；
而在他头顶的苍穹之上，
雄鹰正觅取每日的口粮。

事实与事实相映何其贴切：
英雄与雄鹰，高山与长空。

你们这些蔑养肥了的奴隶的人，
请看这徽记，鼓起勇气吧。

## See in the Print, How Moved by Whim / 看画中，因一时兴起

===Original===
See in the print, how moved by whim
Trumpeting Jumbo, great and grim,
Adjusts his trunk, like a cravat,
To noose that individual's hat.

The sacred Ibis in the distance
Joys to observe his bold resistance.
===Chinese===
看那画中，一时兴起，
大如象、猛如山的詹波昂首鸣啼，
它把长鼻像领带一样盘起，
要把那人的帽子套个结实。

远处那神圣的朱鹭，
欣然观赏它顽强的抗争。

## Mark, Printed on the Opposing Page / 且看对面页上所印

===Original===
Mark, printed on the opposing page,
The unfortunate effects of rage.
A man (who might be you or me)
Hurls another into the sea.

Poor soul, his unreflecting act
His future joys will much contract,
And he will spoil his evening toddy
By dwelling on that mangled body.
===Chinese===
且看对面页上所印，
暴怒招来的不幸后果。
一个人（也许是你或我）
把另一个人抛进了海里。

可怜的人，他这一时莽撞之举，
将大大削减他日后的欢愉，
他还会搅坏傍晚的那杯热酒，
只因为总惦记着那具残破的尸首。

## With Storms A-Weather, Rocks A-Lee / 上风狂飙，下风暗礁

===Original===
With storms a-weather, rocks a-lee,
The dancing skiff puts forth to sea.
The lone dissenter in the blast
Recoils before the sight aghast.

But she, although the heavens be black,
Holds on upon the starboard tack.

For why? although today she sink
Still safe she sails in printers' ink,
And though today the seamen drown,
My cut shall hand their memory down.
===Chinese===
上风是狂风，下风是暗礁，
那轻快的小艇仍出海飘摇。
它是风暴中孤零零的异类，
惊骇地望见此景，直往后退。

可她啊，纵然天色一片漆黑，
依旧稳守右舷的航向不退。

缘何？纵使今日她沉入海中，
仍安然航行于印刷的墨痕里，
纵使今日水手们葬身海底，
我这幅画也会让他们的记忆流传。

## The Careful Angler Chose His Nook / 细心的钓客择了佳处

===Original===
The careful angler chose his nook
At morning by the lilied brook,
And all the noon his rod he plied
By that romantic riverside.

Soon as the evening hours decline
Tranquilly he'll return to dine,
And breathing forth a pious wish,
Will cram his belly full of fish.
===Chinese===
细心的钓客择了佳处，
清早来到百合盛开的溪畔，
整个晌午他都垂钓不倦，
守在那如诗如画的河边。

待到傍晚时分一过，
他便悠然归家用餐，
再虔诚地许下一个心愿，
把满肚子的鱼儿塞得圆满。

## The Abbot for a Walk Went Out / 院长出门散步

===Original===
The Abbot for a walk went out
A wealthy cleric, very stout,
And Robin has that Abbot stuck
As the red hunter spears the buck.

The djavel or the javelin
Has, you observe, gone bravely in,
And you may hear that weapon whack
Bang through the middle of his back.

Hence we may learn that abbots should
Never go walking in a wood.
===Chinese===
院长大人出门散步，
一位富有的教士，十分壮硕，
罗宾却把这位院长一矛刺中，
正如红衣猎人刺杀雄鹿。

那标枪，或是投枪，
你瞧，扎得何等英勇漂亮，
你甚至能听见那兵器"砰"的一声，
正中他后背的当中央。

由此我们当可领悟：
院长大人切莫独自去林中漫步。

## The Frozen Peaks He Once Explored / 他曾踏勘冰封群峰

===Original===
The frozen peaks he once explored,
But now he's dead and by the board.

How better far at home to have stayed
Attended by the parlour maid,
And warmed his knees before the fire
Until the hour when folks retire!

So, if you would be spared to friends.
Do nothing but for business ends.
===Chinese===
他曾踏勘那冰封的群峰，
如今却已死去，葬身海中。

倒不如老老实实待在家中，
有客厅的女仆从旁侍奉，
在炉火前把膝盖烘得暖烘烘，
直守到众人就寝的时辰！

所以，你若想为亲友保全身命，
就凡事只为正经营生而行。

## Industrious Pirate! See Him Sweep / 勤勉的海盗！看他纵横

===Original===
Industrious pirate! see him sweep
The lonely bosom of the deep,
And daily the horizon scan
From Hatteras or Matapan.

Be sure, before that pirate's old,
He will have made a pot of gold,
And will retire from all his labours
And be respected by his neighbors.

You also scan your life's horizon
For all that you can clap your eyes on.
===Chinese===
勤勉的海盗！看他纵横
那寂寥的茫茫海胸，
日复一日地扫视天际，
从哈特拉斯到马塔潘角。

你尽可相信，在这海盗变老之前，
他定会攒下一大罐金元，
从此金盆洗手，不再劳碌，
并赢得邻里乡亲的敬慕。

你也一样，请扫视你人生的天际，
凡目力所及，尽可搜罗无遗。

## A Martial Elegy for Some Lead Soldiers / 致若干铅制士兵的军阵挽歌

===Original===
For certain soldiers lately dead
Our reverent dirge shall here be said.
Them, when their martial leader called,
No dread preparative appalled;
But leaden hearted, leaden heeled,
I marked them steadfast in the field
Death grimly sided with the foe,
And smote each leaden hero low.
Proudly they perished one by one:
The dread Pea-cannon's work was done
O not for them the tears we shed,
Consigned to their congenial lead;
But while unmoved their sleep they take,
We mourn for their dear Captain's sake,
For their dear Captain, who shall smart
Both in his pocket and his heart,
Who saw his heroes shed their gore
And lacked a shilling to buy more!
===Chinese===
为近日阵亡的若干士兵，
我们在此吟诵肃穆的挽歌。
当尚武的统帅将他们召唤，
再骇人的战前准备也未令他们胆寒；
他们铅做的心，铅做的脚跟，
我见他们在战场上岿然不动，
死神冷酷地倒向了敌方，
将每一位铅铸的英雄击倒在地。
他们一个接一个，骄傲地捐躯：
那可怖的豌豆炮已尽其使命，
啊，我们流下的泪水并非为他们，
他们归于与自己同源的铅；
但当他们安然不动地长眠，
我们却为亲爱的统帅而伤感，
为了那位亲爱的统帅，他将痛苦不已，
无论在钱袋上，还是在心底，
他眼看他的英雄们血流如注，
却差一先令，买不起新的兵卒！

## Proem / 序诗

===Original===
Unlike the common run of men,
I wield a double power to please,
And use the Graver and the Pen
With equal aptitude and ease.

I move with that illustrious crew,
The ambidextrous Kings of Art;
And every mortal thing I do
Brings ringing money in the mart.

Hence, to the morning hour, the mead,
The forest and the stream perceive
Me wandering as the muses lead—
Or back returning in the eve.

Two muses like two maiden aunts,
The engraving and the singing muse,
Follow, through all my favorite haunts,
My devious traces in the dews.

To guide and cheer me, each attends;
Each speeds my rapid task along;
One to my cuts her ardour lends,
One breathes her magic in my song.
===Chinese===
不同于寻常凡夫，
我握有取悦他人的双重天赋，
既能操刻刀，又能执笔，
两般技艺皆娴熟自如。

我与那群名流同行，
他们是左右开弓的艺术之王；
凡我经手的一切俗务，
都能在市集上换得叮当作响的钱财。

因此，在清晨的时光，在草甸，
森林与溪流都曾看见
我随缪斯引领而漫游——
或在傍晚时分悠然折返。

两位缪斯，如同两位慈爱的姑妈，
一位司刻版，一位司歌咏，
追随我走过所有钟爱的地方，
循着我沾满露水的蜿蜒行踪。

各有各的陪伴，为我引路助兴；
各有各的法门，催我疾速成事；
一位为我的版画注入热忱，
一位在我的歌声里吐纳神异。

## The Precarious Mill / 摇摇欲坠的磨坊

===Original===
Alone above the stream it stands,
Above the iron hill,
The topsy-turvy, tumble-down,
Yet habitable mill.

Still as the ringing saws advance
To slice the humming deal,
All day the pallid miller hears
The thunder of the wheel.

He hears the river plunge and roar
As roars the angry mob;
He feels the solid building quake,
The trusty timbers throb.

All night beside the fire he cowers:
He hears the rafters jar:
O why is he not in a proper house
As decent people are!

The floors are all aslant, he sees,
The doors are all a-jam;
And from the hook above his head
All crooked swings the ham.

"Alas," he cries and shakes his head,
"I see by every sign,
There soon will be the deuce to pay,
With this estate of mine."
===Chinese===
它孤零零地立于溪流之上，
立于那铁色山丘的顶上，
一座颠倒歪斜、破破烂烂，
却仍勉强可居的磨坊。

随着嘎嘎作响的锯子推进，
切开嗡嗡鸣响的松板，
那面色苍白的磨坊主整日听见
水轮轰鸣，如雷在耳畔。

他听见河水奔涌、咆哮，
如同愤怒的人群呼号；
他感到坚实的建筑在震颤，
可靠的梁木在搏跳。

整夜他蜷缩在炉火旁：
他听见屋椽咯吱作响：
哎，他为何不住一所像样的房子，
像体面人那样！

他瞧见地板处处倾斜，
门扇扇都卡得死死的；
头顶挂钩上挂着的火腿，
也歪歪扭扭地晃个不停。

"哎呀，"他摇头叹气连连，
"我看种种征兆都已分明，
这桩产业很快就要大难临头，
叫我如何收拾得清。"

## The Disputatious Pines / 爱争吵的松树

===Original===
The first pine to the second said:
"My leaves are black, my branches red;
I stand upon this moor of mine,
A hoar, unconquerable pine."

The second sniffed and answered: "Pooh,
I am as good a pine as you."

"Discourteous tree" the first replied,
"The tempest in my boughs had cried,
The hunter slumbered in my shade,
A hundred years ere you were made."

The second smiled as he returned:
"I shall be here when you are burned."

So far dissension ruled the pair,
Each turned on each a frowning air,
When flickering from the bank anigh,
A flight of martens met their eye.

Sometime their course they watched; and then
They nodded off to sleep again.
===Chinese===
第一棵松对第二棵松说：
"我的针叶乌黑，枝干赤红；
我伫立在这片属于我的荒原上，
是一棵苍老、不可征服的松。"

第二棵嗤之以鼻答道："呸，
我是松树，和你一样不亏不亏。"

"无礼的树，"第一棵反驳道，
"暴风雨曾在我的枝头呼啸，
猎人在我的树荫下安睡，
那是一百年前，你还未曾生就。"

第二棵微微一笑，回敬道：
"等你被烧成灰，我仍在此矗立。"

争端至此主宰了这一对，
彼此虎着脸、怒目相对，
忽然从近处的河岸上，
一群燕子翩翩掠过眼前。

它们看了一会儿燕子飞舞；
然后又打着盹，沉沉睡去。

## The Tramps / 流浪汉

===Original===
Now long enough has day endured,
Or King Apollo Palinured,
Seaward be steers his panting team,
And casts on earth his latest gleam.

But see! the Tramps with jaded eye
Their destined provinces espy.
Long through the hills their way they took,
Long camped beside the mountain brook;
'Tis over; now with rising hope
They pause upon the downward slope,

And as their aching bones they rest,
Their anxious captain scans the west.

So paused Alaric on the Alps
And ciphered up the Roman scalps.
===Chinese===
白昼已延续得够久，
阿波罗王，帕利努鲁斯，
正向海边驱赶他喘息的马队，
把最后一缕光辉洒向大地。

且看！流浪汉们带着倦眼，
望见了他们命定的家园。
他们长久地翻山越岭，
长久地在山涧旁扎营；
如今都过去了，怀着渐起的希望，
他们在向下的山坡上驻足张望，

歇着酸痛的筋骨时，
他们焦急的队长正把西天眺望。

当年阿拉里克也曾这样驻足阿尔卑斯山，
清点起那一笔笔罗马的人头账。

## The Foolhardy Geographer / 鲁莽的地理学家

===Original===
The howling desert miles around,
The tinkling brook the only sound—
Wearied with all his toils and feats,
The traveller dines on potted meats;
On potted meats and princely wines,
Not wisely but too well he dines.

The brindled Tiger loud may roar,
High may the hovering Vulture soar,
Alas! regardless of them all,
Soon shall the empurpled glutton sprawl—
Soon, in the desert's hushed repose,
Shall trumpet tidings through his nose!

Alack, unwise! that nasal song
Shall be the Ounce's dinner-gong!

---
A blemish in the cut appears;
Alas! it cost both blood and tears.
The glancing graver swerved aside,
Fast flowed the artist's vital tide!
And now the apologetic bard
Demands indulgence for his pard!
===Chinese===
四围是无尽的啸叫荒漠，
唯一的声响是叮咚的溪流——
他历尽千辛万苦、种种壮举，
这位旅人以罐头肉充饥；
就着罐头肉，饮着王者之酒，
他这顿饭吃得不算明智，却过于丰盛。

斑纹猛虎尽管放声长啸，
盘旋的秃鹫尽管高高翱翔，
唉！他对这一切全不理会，
不久这酒酣耳热的饕客便要横卧——
很快，在荒漠寂静的安歇中，
他将用鼻子吹响号角般的鼾声！

哎呀，糊涂啊！那鼻中的"歌"
将化作雪豹进餐的钟声！

---
画中出现了一处瑕疵；
唉！它耗去了血与泪。
那飞转的刻刀偏了一偏，
艺术家的生命之血流淌如泉！
如今满怀歉意的诗人
为他的搭档请求宽宥！

## The Angler & The Clown / 钓客与小丑

===Original===
The echoing bridge you here may see,
The pouring lynn, the waving tree,
The eager angler fresh from town—
Above, the contumelious clown.

The angler plies his line and rod,
The clodpole stands with many a nod—
With many a nod and many a grin,
He sees him cast his engine in.

"What have you caught?" the peasant cries.

"Nothing as yet," the Fool replies.
===Chinese===
在这里你可以看见回声的桥，
倾泻的瀑布，摇曳的树，
从城里来的热切钓客——
上方，是那个出言不逊的乡巴佬。

钓客一甩钓线和钓竿，
那呆头呆脑的乡巴佬连连点头——
他又是点头又是咧嘴嬉笑，
眼看他把钓具抛进了水波。

"你钓着什么啦？"庄稼汉喊道。

"这会儿还什么都没有。"傻瓜答道。

## Robin and Ben / 罗宾与本

*Or, The Pirate and the Apothecary*

===Original===
Come lend me an attentive ear
A startling moral tale to hear,
Of Pirate Rob and Chemist Ben,
And different destinies of men.

Deep in the greenest of the vales
That nestle near the coast of Wales,
The heaving main but just in view,
Robin and Ben together grew,
Together worked and played the fool,
Together shunned the Sunday school,
And pulled each other's youthful noses
Around the cots, among the roses.

Together but unlike they grew;
Robin was rough, and through and through
Bold, inconsiderate, and manly,
Like some historic Bruce or Stanley.
Ben had a mean and servile soul,
He robbed not, though he often stole.
He sang on Sunday in the choir,
And tamely capped the passing Squire.

At length, intolerant of trammels—
Wild as the wild Bithynian camels,
Wild as the wild sea-eagles—Bob
His widowed dam contrives to rob,
And thus with great originality
Effectuates his personality.
Thenceforth his terror-haunted flight
He follows through the starry night;
And with the early morning breeze,
Behold him on the azure seas.
The master of a trading dandy
Hires Robin for a go of brandy;
And all the happy hills of home
Vanish beyond the fields of foam.

Ben, meanwhile, like a tin reflector,
Attended on the worthy rector;
Opened his eyes and held his breath,
And flattered to the point of death;
And was at last, by that good fairy,
Apprenticed to the Apothecary.

So Ben, while Robin chose to ro
A rising chemist was at home,
Tended his shop with learnèd air,
Watered his drugs and oiled his hair,
And gave advice to the unwary,
Like any sleek apothecary.

Meanwhile upon the deep afar
Robin the brave was waging war,
With other tarry desperadoes
About the latitude of Barbados.
He knew no touch of craven fear;
His voice was thunder in the cheer;
First, from the main-to'-gallan' high,
The skulking merchantman to spy—
The first to bound upon the deck,
The last to leave the sinking wreck.
His hand was steel, his word was law,
His mates regarded him with awe.
No pirate in the whole profession
Held a more honourable position.

At length, from years of anxious toil,
Bold Robin seeks his native soil;
Wisely arranges his affairs,
And to his native dale repairs.
The Bristol Swallow* sets him down
Beside the well-remembered town.
He sighs, he spits, he marks the scene,
Proudly he treads the village green;
And free from pettiness and rancour,
Takes lodgings at the Crown and Anchor.

Strange when a man so great and good,
Once more in his home-country stood,
Strange that the sordid clowns should show
A dull desire to have him go.

His clinging breeks, his tarry hat,
The way he swore, the way he spat,
A certain quality of manner,
Alarming like the pirate's banner—
Something that did not seem to suit all—
Something, O call it bluff, not brutal—
Something at least, howe'er it's called,
Made Robin generally black-balled.

His soul was wounded; proud and glum,
Alone he sat and swigged his rum,
And took a great distaste to men
Till he encountered Chemist Ben.
Bright was the hour and bright the day,
That threw them in each other's way;
Glad were their mutual salutations,
Long their respective revelations.
Before the inn in sultry weather
They talked of this and that together;
Ben told the tale of his indentures,
And Rob narrated his adventures.
Last, as the point of greatest weight,
The pair contrasted their estate,
And Robin, like a boastful sailor,
Despised the other for a tailor.

"See," he remarked, "with envy, see
A man with such a fist as me!
Bearded and ringed, and big, and brown,
I sit and toss the stingo down.
Hear the gold jingle in my bag—
All won beneath the Jolly Flag!"

Ben moralised and shook his head:
"You wanderers earn and eat your bread.
The foe is found, beats or is beaten,
And either how, the wage is eaten.
And after all your pully-hauly
Your proceeds look uncommon small-ly.
You had done better here to tarry
Apprentice to the Apothecary.
The silent pirates of the shore
Eat and sleep soft, and pocket more
Than any red, robustious ranger
Who picks his farthings hot from danger.
You clank your guineas on the board;
Mine are with several bankers stored.
You reckon riches on your digits,
You dash in chase of Sals and Bridgets,
You drink and risk delirium tremens,
Your whole estate a common seaman's!
Regard your friend and school companion,
Soon to be wed to Miss Trevanion
(Smooth, honourable, fat and flowery,
With Heaven knows how much land in dowry)
Look at me—am I in good case?
Look at my hands, look at my face;
Look at the cloth of my apparel;
Try me and test me, lock and barrel;
And own, to give the devil his due,
I have made more of life than you.
Yet I nor sought nor risked a life;
I shudder at an open knife;
The perilous seas I still avoided
And stuck to land whate'er betided.
I had no gold, no marble quarry,
I was a poor apothecary,
Yet here I stand, at thirty-eight,
A man of an assured estate."

"Well," answered Robin—"well, and how?"

The smiling chemist tapped his brow.
"Rob," he replied, "this throbbing brain
Still worked and hankered after gain.
By day and night, to work my will,
It pounded like a powder mill;
And marking how the world went round
A theory of theft it found.
Here is the key to right and wrong:
Steal little but steal all day long;
And this invaluable plan
Marks what is called the Honest Man.
When first I served with Doctor Pill,
My hand was ever in the till.
Now that I am myself a master
My gains come softer still and faster.
As thus: on Wednesday, a maid
Came to me in the way of trade.
Her mother, an old farmer's wife,
Required a drug to save her life.
'At once, my dear, at once,' I said,
Patted the child upon the head,
Bade her be still a loving daughter,
And filled the bottle up with water.

"Well, and the mother?" Robin cried.

"O she!" said Ben, "I think she died."

"Battle and blood, death and disease,
Upon the tainted Tropic seas—
The attendant sharks that chew the cud—
The abhorred scuppers spouting blood—
The untended dead, the Tropic sun—
The thunder of the murderous gun—
The cut-throat crew—the Captain's curse—
The tempest blustering worse and worse—
These have I known and these can stand,
But you, I settle out of hand!"

Out flashed the cutlass, down went
Dead and rotten, there and then.
===Chinese===
来，且倾耳细听，
听一段发人深省的奇闻，
说的是海盗罗宾与药剂师本，
以及人各有命、各有归途。

在威尔士海岸边那片
最青翠的幽谷深处，
海上波涛隐隐可望，
罗宾与本一同长大，
一同劳作，一同胡闹，
一同逃学，不去上主日学校，
还在农舍边、玫瑰丛中，
你揪我的鼻子，我揪你的鼻子。

两人一同长大，却性情迥异；
罗宾粗豪，骨子里透着
胆大、莽撞，一派男子气，
有如史册中的布鲁斯或斯坦利。
本却生就一副卑贱奴颜，
他不曾抢劫，却常常偷窃。
他在礼拜日的唱诗班里歌唱，
遇见路过的乡绅，便唯唯诺诺地点头哈腰。

终于，他再也无法忍受束缚——
像比提尼亚的野骆驼般狂野，
像海上的野雕般桀骜——鲍勃
设法偷走了他那守寡的老母的钱财，
于是以非凡的独创性，
确立了他独立的个性。
从此他满怀被恐惧追逐的逃亡，
追随繁星，奔走于长夜；
待到清晨的微风吹起，
看哪，他已航行在碧蓝的海上。
一艘单桅商船的船长
雇佣罗宾，换取一顿白兰地；
于是故乡那些幸福的山峦，
都消逝在浪花的田野之外。

本呢，像一面锡制的反光镜，
侍奉着那位可敬的教区长；
他瞪大眼睛，屏住呼吸，
一味谄媚，直到把人捧上天去；
终于，多亏那位好心的仙女，
他被送去给药剂师当了学徒。

于是当罗宾选择了四海漂泊，
本在家里成了个崛起的药剂师，
他以满腹学问的姿态打理店铺，
给他的药材浇水，给他的头发抹油，
像任何一位油光水滑的药剂师那样，
向不知就里的人兜售忠告。

与此同时，在遥远的碧海之上，
英勇的罗宾正征战不休，
与一帮满身柏油的亡命之徒
出没在巴巴多斯一带的纬度。
他不知怯懦为何物；
他的声音是欢呼时的雷霆；
他第一个攀上主桅顶桁的高处，
去窥探那躲躲闪闪的商船——
他第一个跃上敌船的甲板，
最后一个离开沉没的残骸。
他的手是钢铁，他的话是律令，
他的伙伴们对他敬畏交加。
在整个海盗这一行当中，
没有谁的地位比他更为体面。

终于，历经多年焦虑的辛劳，
英勇的罗宾回归故土；
他明智地料理好一切事务，
回到了他出生的山谷。
布里斯托尔的"燕子号"*载他
降落在那座记忆犹新的小镇。
他叹气，他吐痰，他打量眼前的景象，
他骄傲地踏上村中的草地；
他胸怀坦荡，不带半点芥蒂与怨怼，
径直在"王冠与船锚"客栈住了下来。

奇怪啊，一个如此伟大、如此善良的人，
重新屹立于他的故乡之土，
奇怪的是那些鄙俗的乡下佬
竟显出一种迟钝的、要他离开的意思。

他紧紧裹在身上的马裤，他那沾满柏油的帽子，
他骂人的腔调，他吐痰的做派，
还有他举止中某种说不清的气质，
令人心惊，宛如海盗的旗幡——
某种似乎与众人都不相称的东西——
某种，啊，姑且称之为粗豪，而非残暴的东西——
某种至少，无论怎么称呼的东西，
使得罗宾处处遭到白眼与排挤。

他的自尊受了伤；他闷闷不乐，孤高自赏，
独自坐着，灌着他的朗姆酒，
对人产生了莫大的厌弃，
直到他遇见了药剂师本。
那一刻何等明亮，那一日何等灿烂，
命运把他们抛到了彼此眼前；
他们的寒暄何等热切，
各自的倾吐又何等漫长。
在那闷热的天气里，客栈门前，
他们天南海北地聊了起来；
本讲了他当学徒的往事，
罗宾讲了他的海上奇遇。
最后，作为压轴的话题，
两人比起了彼此的身家，
而罗宾，像个爱吹嘘的水手，
蔑视对方，视其为裁缝之流。

"瞧，"他说，"且怀着艳羡瞧一瞧，
一个长着这样一双重拳的人！
胡须满脸，戒指满手，又高又黑，
我坐在这儿，把烈酒一杯杯灌下。
听我钱袋里的金币叮当作响——
都是在骷髅旗下赢来的赏银！"

本却摇摇头，搬出一番大道理：
"你们这些浪迹天涯的人，挣了面包也吃了面包。
找到了敌人，或打败对方，或被对方打败，
无论如何，那点酬劳都被吃个精光。
任凭你们在甲板上吆五喝六，
到头来进项却少得可怜兮兮。
你们倒不如老老实实待在这里，
给药剂师当个学徒。
海岸上那些无声的海盗，
吃得香，睡得软，口袋里装的
比任何红脸膛、莽撞冲动的游侠
从虎口里一分一厘抠出来的都多。
你把几尼金币叮当敲在桌上；
我的呢，存放在好几家银行里。
你掰着手指头数家当，
你撒腿去追萨莉和布丽吉特那些姑娘，
你酗酒，冒着震颤性谵妄的风险，
你全部家当不过一个普通水手的行囊！
瞧瞧你的朋友、你的同窗，
眼看就要迎娶特雷文尼安小姐
（又圆滑、又体面、又肥胖、又花团锦簇，
带着天知道多少亩的嫁妆）
你看看我——我气色可好？
你看看我的手，再看看我的脸；
你看看我身上衣料的质地；
你不妨里里外外考校我一番；
然后承认，把魔鬼应得的归给魔鬼，
我这辈子活得比你强。
可我既不曾寻衅，也不曾冒险；
我一见明晃晃的刀子就发抖；
我始终远避那凶险的海洋，
无论发生什么，都守在陆地之上。
我既没有黄金，也没有大理石矿，
我曾是个穷光蛋药剂师，
可如今我站在这里，三十八岁，
是一个身家稳固的有产者。"

"好吧，"罗宾答道——"好吧，然后呢？"

那笑容可掬的药剂师敲了敲自己的额头。
"罗伯，"他答道，"这颗砰砰跳动的大脑
从不歇息，一味渴求着钱财。
日日夜夜，为遂我心愿，
它像火药磨坊般轰隆作响；
它留心观察这世界运转的规律，
悟出了一套关于偷盗的理论。
这便是辨别是非善恶的钥匙：
偷得少一点，但要整天偷个不停；
而这套无价之宝般的计划，
恰恰造就了所谓"老实人"。
当初我跟着皮尔大夫学徒的时候，
我的手就没离开过钱箱。
如今我自己当了老板，
进项来得更柔顺、更飞快。
比如这样：某个星期三，
有个姑娘循着生意上门。
她的母亲，一个老农的妻子，
急需一味药来救命。
'马上就来，亲爱的，马上就来，'我说，
一边拍了拍那孩子的头，
嘱咐她要继续做个孝顺女儿，
然后我把瓶子里灌满了清水。"

"那么，那位母亲呢？"罗宾喊道。

"哦，她呀！"本说，"我想她是死了。"

"刀光剑影、鲜血淋漓、死亡与疫病，
在那污浊的热带海洋之上——
那些埋头反刍的随行鲨鱼——
那些令人作呕、喷着血水的排水孔——
无人收殓的死者、热带的烈日——
那凶残杀戮的火炮雷霆——
那帮杀人越货的亡命徒——船长的咒骂——
那愈刮愈烈、狂暴不堪的风暴——
这些我都见识过，这些我都挺得住，
可你这种人，我当下就了断！"

弯刀一出鞘，扑通一声，
当场倒下，死透烂透。

## The Builder's Doom / 建筑者的报应

===Original===
In eighteen twenty Deacon Thin
Feu'd the land and fenced it in,
And laid his broad foundations down
About a furlong out of town.

Early and late the work went on.
The carts were toiling ere the dawn;
The mason whistled, the hodman sang;
Early and late the trowels rang;
And Thin himself came day by day
To push the work in every way.

An artful builder, patent king
Of all the local building ring,
Who was there like him in the quarter
For mortifying brick and mortar,
Or pocketing the odd piastre
By substituting lath and plaster?
With plan and two-foot rule in hand,
He by the foreman took his stand,
With boisterous voice, with eagle glance
To stamp upon extravagance.
Far thrift of bricks and greed of guilders,
He was the Bonaparte of Builders.

The foreman, a desponding creature,
Demurred to here and there a feature:
"For surely, sir—with your permeesion—
Bricks here, sir, in the main parteetion…"
The builder goggled, gulped and stared,
The foreman's services were spared.
Thin would not count among his minions
A man of Wesleyan opinions.

"Money is money," so he said.
"Crescents are crescents, trade is trade.
Pharaohs and emperors in their seasons
Built, I believe, for different reasons—
Charity, glory, piety, pride—
To pay the men, to please a bride,
To use their stone, to spite their neighbours,
Not for a profit on their labours.
They built to edify or bewilder;
I build because I am a builder.
Crescent and street and square I build,
Plaster and paint and carve and gild.
Around the city see them stand,
These triumphs of my shaping hand,
With bulging walls, with sinking floors,
With shut, impracticable doors,
Fickle and frail in every part,
And rotten to their inmost heart.
There shall the simple tenant find
Death in the falling window-blind,
Death in the pipe, death in the faucet,
Death in the deadly water-closet!
A day is set for all to die:
*Caveat emptor!* what care I?"

As to Amphion's tuneful kit
Troy rose, with towers encircling it;
As to the Mage's brandished wand
A spiry palace clove the sand;
To Thin's indomitable financing,
That phantom crescent kept advancing.
When first the brazen bells of churches
Called clerk and parson to their perches,
The worshippers of every sect
Already viewed it with respect;
A second Sunday had not gone
Before the roof was rattled on:
And when the fourth was there, behold
The crescent finished, painted, sold!

The stars proceeded in their courses,
Nature with her subversive forces,
Time, too, the iron-toothed and sinewed;
And the edacious years continued.
Thrones rose and fell; and still the crescent,
Unsanative and now senescent,
A plastered skeleton of lath,
Looked forward to a day of wrath.
In the dead night, the groaning timber
Would jar upon the ear of slumber,
And, like Dodona's talking oak,
Of oracles and judgments spoke.
When to the music fingered well
The feet of children lightly fell,
The sire, who dozed by the decanters,
Started, and dreamed of misadventures.
The rotten brick decayed to dust;
The iron was consumed by rust;
Each tabid and perverted mansion
Hung in the article of declension.

So forty, fifty, sixty passed;
Until, when seventy came at last,
The occupant of number three
Called friends to hold a jubilee.
Wild was the night; the charging rack
Had forced the moon upon her back;
The wind piped up a naval ditty;
And the lamps winked through all the city.
Before that house, where lights were shining,
Corpulent feeders, grossly dining,
And jolly clamour, hum and rattle,
Fairly outvoiced the tempest's battle.
As still his moistened lip he fingered,
The envious policeman lingered;
While far the infernal tempest sped,
And shook the country folks in bed,
And tore the trees and tossed the ships,
He lingered and he licked his lips.
Lo, from within, a hush! the host
Briefly expressed the evening's toast;
And lo, before the lips were dry,
The Deacon rising to reply!

"Here in this house which once I built,
Papered and painted, carved and gilt,
And out of which, to my content,
I netted seventy-five percent;
Here at this board of jolly neighbours,
I reap the credit of my labours.
These were the days—I will say more—
These were the grand old days of yore!
The builder laboured day and night;
He watched that every brick was right;
The decent men their utmost did;
And the house rose—a pyramid!
These were the days, our provost knows,
When forty streets and crescents rose,
The fruits of my creative noddle,
All more or less upon a model,
Neat and commodious, cheap and dry,
A perfect pleasure to the eye!
I found this quite a country quarter;
I leave it solid lath and mortar.
In all, I was the single actor—
And am this city's benefactor!
Since then, alas! both thing and name,
Shoddy across the ocean came—
Shoddy that can the eye bewilder
And makes me blush to meet a builder!
Had this good house, in frame or fixture,
Been tempered by the least admixture
Of that discreditable shoddy,
Should we today compound our toddy,
Or gaily marry song and laughter
Below its sempiternal rafter?
Not so!" the Deacon cried.

The mansion
Had marked his fatuous expansion.
The years were full, the house was fated,
The rotten structure crepitated!
A moment, and the silent guests
Sat pallid as their dinner vests.

A moment more, and root and branch,
That mansion fell in avalanche,
Story on story, floor on floor,
Roof, wall and window, joist and door,
Dead weight of damnable disaster,
A cataclysm of lath and plaster.

Siloam did not choose a sinner—
All were not builders at the dinner.
===Chinese===
一八二零年，执事锡恩
买下地皮，把它圈了起来，
在城外约一弗隆的地方，
打下了他宽阔的地基。

工程不分早晚地赶。
大车在天亮前就开始辚辚；
石匠吹着口哨，搬砖工唱着小调；
抹泥的瓦刀从早到晚叮当作响；
而锡恩本人也日日到场，
想尽办法催促工程进展。

一个精明的建筑商，当地建筑行当里
独占鳌头的王者，
在这一方有谁能与他相比——
论折磨砖瓦泥灰的本事，
论偷梁换柱、以板条灰膏蒙混过关，
从而把那几个零碎银币揣进腰包？
他一手拿着图纸，一手拿着两英尺长的矩尺，
往工头身旁一站，
嗓门粗豪，目光如鹰，
专门压制一切铺张浪费。
他对砖块的吝惜和对镀金匠的贪婪，无人能及，
他是建筑界的拿破仑。

那工头，一个垂头丧气的可怜虫，
对这样那样的细节提出异议：
"先生，恕我直言——冒昧得很——
这儿，先生，主隔墙里头该用砖头……"
建筑商瞪眼、咽口水、目瞪口呆，
工头随即被解雇了事。
锡恩不愿在他的麾下
留用一个信奉卫斯理宗的人。

"钱就是钱，"他如是说。
"新月楼就是新月楼，生意就是生意。
历代的法老和帝王，在他们的时节里，
我相信，是出于不同的缘由而大兴土木——
为了行善、为了荣耀、为了虔诚、为了骄傲——
为了给工人发工钱，为了取悦新娘，
为了用掉自家的石材，为了跟邻居斗气，
却不是为了在劳工身上赚取利润。
他们盖楼，是为了教化人心或令人惊叹；
我盖楼，是因为我是个盖楼的。
新月楼、街道、广场，都是我盖的，
抹灰、刷漆、雕花、贴金，样样精通。
看看它们矗立在城市四周，
这些都是我双手造就的丰碑，
墙壁鼓胀，地板下陷，
门窗紧闭，难以开启，
处处三心二意、脆弱不堪，
从表皮到芯子，全都腐烂透顶。
在那里，单纯可怜的租客将发现
死神藏在落下的窗帘里，
死神藏在管子里，死神藏在龙头里，
死神藏在要命的马桶里！
万物皆有一死：
*货品售出，概不退换！*关我何事？"

正如安菲翁悠扬的里拉琴声
唤起了特洛伊，城池环绕、巍然耸立；
正如术士挥动魔杖，
一座尖塔林立的宫殿从沙中拔地而起；
靠着锡恩百折不挠的融资手段，
那座虚无缥缈的新月楼也节节拔高、不断推进。
当教堂的铜钟头一回敲响，
把书记员和牧师召唤到他们的讲坛之上，
各宗各派的信众
已经对它心怀敬仰；
第二个礼拜天还没过完，
屋顶就已经叮叮当当地盖了上去：
到了第四个礼拜天，你瞧，
新月楼已经竣工、粉刷、卖出去了！

星辰沿着各自的轨道运行，
大自然施展着她那颠覆一切的力量，
时间，那铁齿钢筋的时间，
还有那吞噬一切的年月，也不曾停歇。
王座起起落落；而那座新月楼，
无可救药，如今也已老朽，
一具板条搭就、糊着灰膏的骷髅，
静待着审判之日的到来。
在死寂的深夜，那呻吟的木料
会惊扰酣睡者的耳朵，
又如多多纳那棵会说话的橡树，
讲述着神谕与报应。
当手指娴熟地弹奏起乐曲，
孩子们的脚丫轻快地踩着节拍，
那位在酒瓶旁打盹的老爷，
会猛然惊醒，梦见种种灾祸。
烂砖化作了尘土；
铁件被锈蚀吞噬；
每一座病入膏肓、走了样的宅邸
都摇摇欲坠，濒临坍塌。

就这样，四十、五十、六十年过去了；
直到七十年来临，
三号楼的住户
邀集友人，举办一场庆典。
那一夜风狂雨骤；翻滚的乱云
逼得月亮仰面朝天；
风儿吹起了一支水手号子；
全城的灯火在风中明明灭灭。
在那座灯火通明的宅子前，
大腹便便的食客们正在大快朵颐，
欢声笑语、喧哗嘈杂，
竟盖过了暴风雨的咆哮。
那个嫉妒的警察在那儿流连不去，
一边不住地用舌头舔着湿润的嘴唇；
而那可怖的风暴席卷到远方，
把乡下的农人震得在床上发抖，
撕扯着树木，掀翻着船舶，
他却赖在那儿，舔了又舔他的嘴唇。
看哪，屋内忽然一片肃静！主人
简短地举杯祝酒；
看哪，祝酒的嘴唇还未干透，
执事大人已经起身答谢！

"在这座房子里——它曾由我亲手建造，
裱糊、油漆、雕花、贴金，
而我从中，心满意足地
净赚了百分之七十五的利润；
坐在这桌欢快的乡邻之间，
我收获着我辛劳的荣光。
那是个好时代——我还要说——
那是个了不起的、往昔的黄金时代！
建筑者日夜操劳；
他盯着每一块砖都安放妥帖；
体面的人们都竭尽了全力；
于是房子拔地而起——一座金字塔！
那是个好时代，我们的镇长也晓得，
那时有四十条街道和新月楼拔地而起，
全是我这颗富于创造力的脑瓜的果实，
多多少少都遵循同一个模型，
整洁、合用、便宜、干燥，
看着简直赏心悦目！
我来时，这里还是一片乡野；
我走时，已是一片结结实实的板条和灰泥。
总之，从头到尾只有我一个功臣——
而我，就是这座城市的恩人！
打那以后，唉！无论是物还是名，
那种叫'劣质再生毛'的东西漂洋过海传了过来——
那种东西能把人的眼睛都看花，
让我如今羞于见同行！
要是这座好房子，无论是在骨架上还是在装修上，
掺进了哪怕一丁点儿
那种丢人现眼的劣质货，
我们今天还能在这里调配热潘趣酒吗？
还能在它那永恒不朽的屋梁之下，
尽情地载歌载舞、欢声笑语吗？
决不至于此！"执事大人高声道。

那座宅邸
印证了他那荒唐的膨胀。
年岁已满，宅邸气数已尽，
那腐朽的结构开始嘎嘎作响！
一瞬间，满座无言的宾客
脸白得像他们身上的餐礼服。

再过片刻，连根带枝，
那座宅邸便如雪崩般坍塌，
一层叠着一层，一楼压着一楼，
屋顶、墙壁、窗户、托梁、门扇，
全是那该死的灾祸的死沉重量，
一场板条与灰泥的大洪流。

西罗亚塔并没有专挑一个罪人——
因为赴宴的人里，并非个个都是建筑商。

## To Alison Cunningham / 致艾莉森·坎宁安

*From Her Boy / 她的孩子敬献*

===Original===
For the long nights you lay awake
And watched for my unworthy sake:
For your most comfortable hand
That led me through the uneven land:
For all the story-books you read:
For all the pains you comforted:
For all you pitied, all you bore,
In sad and happy days of yore:—
My second Mother, my first Wife,
The angel of my infant life—
From the sick child, now well and old,
Take, nurse, the little book you hold!

And grant it, Heaven, that all who read
May find as dear a nurse at need,
And every child who lists my rhyme,
In the bright, fireside, nursery clime,
May hear it in as kind a voice
As made my childish days rejoice!
===Chinese===
为了你为我这不争气的孩子，
彻夜难眠、守望不歇的那些长夜：
为了你那双最令人安心的手，
牵引我走过坎坷不平的土地：
为了你念给我听的那些故事书：
为了你抚慰过的种种病痛：
为了你所怜惜的、所承受的一切，
无论在忧愁还是欢愉的往昔岁月里：——
我的第二位母亲，我的发妻，
我襁褓岁月里的天使——
如今病儿已愈，且已年迈，
收下吧，保姆，你手中这卷小书！

并祈求上苍，愿所有读到此书的人，
在需要时都能遇上一位如此亲爱的保姆，
愿每一个聆听我这诗篇的孩子，
在那明亮的、炉火旁的、育婴室的天地里，
都能用同样温柔的声音听见它，
一如那曾令我童年欢欣的声音！

## Bed in Summer / 夏日的床

===Original===
In winter I get up at night
And dress by yellow candlelight.
In summer, quite the other way—
I have to go to bed by day.
I have to go to bed and see
The birds still hopping on the tree,
Or hear the grown-up people's feet
Still going past me in the street.
And does it not seem hard to you,
When all the sky is clear and blue,
And I should like so much to play,
To have to go to bed by day?
===Chinese===
冬天里我半夜就得起床，
借着昏黄的烛光穿衣裳。
夏天里，却恰好相反——
我得天还没黑就上床。
我得上床去，眼睁睁看着
鸟儿还在树上蹦蹦跳跳，
或是听着大人们的脚步声
还在街上从我身旁走过。
你不觉得这太叫人为难吗，
当满天晴空一片蔚蓝，
而我又是多么想出去玩，
却不得不在大白天里上床？

## A Thought / 一个念头

===Original===
It is very nice to think
The world is full of meat and drink,
With little children saying grace
In every Christian kind of place.
===Chinese===
想着想着就觉着真好，
这世界到处是吃的和喝的，
小小孩子们在每一个
信奉基督的地方谢饭祷告。

## At the Sea-Side / 在海滨

===Original===
When I was down beside the sea,
A wooden spade they gave to me
To dig the sandy shore.
My holes were empty like a cup,
In every hole the sea came up,
Till it could come no more.
===Chinese===
当我来到大海边上，
他们给了我一把小木铲，
让我去挖那沙滩。
我挖的洞空得像只杯子，
海水涌进了每一个洞里，
直到再也装不下半点。

## Young Night Thought / 小小夜思

===Original===
All night long, and every night,
When my mamma puts out the light,
I see the people marching by,
As plain as day, before my eye.

Armies and emperors and kings,
All carrying different kinds of things,
And marching in so grand a way,
You never saw the like by day.

So fine a show was never seen
At the great circus on the green;
For every kind of beast and man
Is marching in that caravan.

At first they move a little slow,
But still the faster on they go,
And still beside them close I keep
Until we reach the town of Sleep.

===Chinese===
每个夜晚，夜夜如此，
妈妈一灭灯，我就看见，
人们排着队伍走过去，
清清楚楚，就在我眼前。

军队、皇帝，还有国王，
手里拿着各色各样的行装，
走得那么威风、那么排场，
白天你从没见过这模样。

这么好看的场面你从没瞧见，
哪怕草地上的大马戏团；
因为各种各样的野兽和人，
都走在这支游行队伍间。

起先他们走得有点儿慢，
可后来越走越快、越走越欢，
我就紧紧地跟在他们身边，
直到我们走进那座瞌睡城。

## Whole Duty of Children / 孩子的本分

===Original===
A child should always say what’s true,
And speak when he is spoken to,
And behave mannerly at table:
At least as far as he is able.

===Chinese===
一个孩子应当总说真话，
别人跟他说话他才回答，
吃饭的时候要斯文有礼：
至少，尽他自己所能做到的。

## Rain / 雨

===Original===
The rain is raining all around,
It falls on field and tree,
It rains on the umbrellas here,
And on the ships at sea.

===Chinese===
大雨哗哗四处下，
落在田野和树上，
雨打这里的雨伞，
也打海上的船。

## Pirate Story / 海盗的故事

===Original===
Three of us afloat in the meadow by the swing,
Three of us aboard in the basket on the lea.
Winds are in the air, they are blowing in the spring,
And waves are on the meadow like the waves there are at sea.

Where shall we adventure, today that we’re afloat,
Wary of the weather, and steering by a star?
Shall it be to Africa, a-steering of the boat,
To Providence, or Babylon, or off to Malabar?

Hi! but here’s a squadron a-rowing on the sea—
Cattle on the meadow a-charging with a roar!
Quick, and we’ll escape them, they’re as mad as they can be,
The wicket is the harbour and the garden is the shore.

===Chinese===
我们仨在草地秋千旁，船儿水上漂，
我们仨在草坡篮子里，当成了大船。
风在天上吹，春风正闹得欢，
草地上的波浪，就跟海上一个样。

今天我们漂在海上，去哪儿闯一闯？
一边提防着天气，一边望着星星掌舵？
是驾着小船去非洲逛一逛，
还是去普罗维登斯、巴比伦，或者马拉巴尔？

嘿！可这儿有一队战船，正在海上划着——
草地上的牛群，轰隆隆地冲过来啦！
快，我们赶紧躲开，它们气得没法说，
小栅门就是港口，花园就是海岸。

## Foreign Lands / 异乡

===Original===
Up into the cherry-tree
Who should climb but little me?
I held the trunk with both my hands
And looked abroad on foreign lands.

I saw the next-door garden lie,
Adorned with flowers, before my eye,
And many pleasant places more
That I had never seen before.

I saw the dimpling river pass
And be the sky’s blue looking-glass;
The dusty roads go up and down
With people tramping in to town.

If I could find a higher tree,
Farther and farther I should see
To where the grown-up river slips
Into the sea among the ships,

To where the roads on either hand
Lead onward into fairyland,
Where all the children dine at five,
And all the playthings come alive.

===Chinese===
爬上那棵樱桃树，
除了小小的我还有谁？
我双手紧紧抱着树干，
向远方眺望异乡的风光。

我看见隔壁家的花园，
在我眼前铺开，开满鲜花，
还有许多好看好玩的地方，
都是我以前从未见过的呀。

我看见那条泛着酒窝的小河流过，
当成了天空蓝色的镜子；
尘土飞扬的路上高高低低，
人们正踩着步子走进城里。

要是我能找到一棵更高的树，
我就能看得更远、更远，
看见那条长大了的大河，
溜进海里，钻进船队中间，

看见两旁的大路小路，
一直通向那童话王国，
那儿的孩子五点就吃饭，
所有的玩具都会活起来。

## Windy Nights / 风之夜

===Original===
Whenever the moon and stars are set,
Whenever the wind is high,
All night long in the dark and wet,
A man goes riding by.
Late in the night when the fires are out,
Why does he gallop and gallop about?

Whenever the trees are crying aloud,
And ships are tossed at sea,
By, on the highway, low and loud,
By at the gallop goes he.
By at the gallop he goes, and then
By he comes back at the gallop again.

===Chinese===
每当月亮和星星都落下，
每当大风呼呼地刮，
一整夜，在又黑又湿的夜里，
就有个人骑着马经过。
夜深了，家家灯火都已熄灭，
他为什么还策马奔来奔去？

每当树林呜呜地哭喊，
海上的船只被抛上抛下，
他从大路上经过，低一声、响一声，
他策马疾驰地经过。
他策马疾驰地一掠而过，
接着又策马疾驰奔回来啦。

## Travel / 远行

===Original===
I should like to rise and go
Where the golden apples grow;—
Where below another sky
Parrot islands anchored lie,
And, watched by cockatoos and goats,
Lonely Crusoes building boats;—
Where in sunshine reaching out
Eastern cities, miles about,
Are with mosque and minaret
Among sandy gardens set,
And the rich goods from near and far
Hang for sale in the bazaar;—
Where the Great Wall round China goes,
And on one side the desert blows,
And with bell and voice and drum,
Cities on the other hum;—
Where are forests, hot as fire,
Wide as England, tall as a spire,
Full of apes and coconuts
And the negro hunters’ huts;—
Where the knotty crocodile
Lies and blinks in the Nile,
And the red flamingo flies
Hunting fish before his eyes;—
Where in jungles, near and far,
Man-devouring tigers are,
Lying close and giving ear
Lest the hunt be drawing near,
Or a comer-by be seen
Swinging in a palanquin;—
Where among the desert sands
Some deserted city stands,
All its children, sweep and prince,
Grown to manhood ages since,
Not a foot in street or house,
Nor a stir of child or mouse,
And when kindly falls the night,
In all the town no spark of light.
There I’ll come when I’m a man
With a camel caravan;
Light a fire in the gloom
Of some dusty dining-room;
See the pictures on the walls,
Heroes, fights, and festivals;
And in a corner find the toys
Of the old Egyptian boys.

===Chinese===
我真想动身，远走他乡，
去那金苹果生长的地方；——
那里，在另一片天空之下，
停泊着一个个鹦鹉岛，
鹦鹉和山羊在四处张望，
孤单的鲁滨孙正在造船；——
那里，在灿烂的阳光里，
东方的城池绵延好几里，
清真寺和细细的尖塔，
立在一片片沙土花园中，
从四面八方运来的好东西，
都挂在集市上等着卖；——
那里，长城绕着中国蜿蜒，
一边是沙漠黄沙漫天，
另一边的城池却热闹喧响，
钟声、人声、鼓声一片；——
那里，有大片森林热得像火，
像英格兰一样宽，像塔尖一样高，
里面满是猿猴和椰子，
还有黑皮肤猎人的茅屋；——
那里，满身疙瘩的鳄鱼，
趴在尼罗河里眨巴眼睛，
红色的火烈鸟飞过眼前，
正盯着鱼儿想捉来尝尝；——
那里，在丛林深处，远远近近，
都有吃人的猛虎出没，
它们悄悄趴着，竖起耳朵，
生怕猎人正在逼近，
或者有路人正坐着轿子，
一摇一晃地从林间经过；——
那里，在茫茫黄沙之中，
立着一座被遗弃的空城，
城里的孩子，从扫地童到王子，
早在多少个世纪前就已长大成人，
街上、屋里没有一只脚在走动，
连孩子和老鼠都没有一丝声响，
等温柔的夜幕落下来，
整座城里没有一点灯火亮。
等我长大了，我就要去那儿，
跟着一支骆驼商队同行；
在那满是灰尘的餐厅里，
黑咕隆咚地点起一盏灯火；
看看墙上的那些图画，
英雄、战斗、还有节庆；
再在一个角落里找到，
那些古埃及男孩的玩具。

## Singing / 歌声

===Original===
Of speckled eggs the birdie sings
And nests among the trees;
The sailor sings of ropes and things
In ships upon the seas.

The children sing in far Japan,
The children sing in Spain;
The organ with the organ man
Is singing in the rain.

===Chinese===
小鸟唱着带斑点的鸟蛋，
还有树林里的窝巢；
水手唱着缆绳和别的，
在茫茫大海的船上。

遥远的日本，孩子们在唱歌，
西班牙的孩子也在唱；
手风琴跟着琴手，
正在雨里叮叮当当。

## Looking Forward / 向往

===Original===
When I am grown to man’s estate
I shall be very proud and great,
And tell the other girls and boys
Not to meddle with my toys.

===Chinese===
等我长大成了大人，
我会又神气、又了不起，
告诉别的女孩子和男孩子，
别来碰我的玩具。

## A Good Play / 一场好玩的游戏

===Original===
We built a ship upon the stairs
All made of the back-bedroom chairs
And filled it full of sofa pillows
To go a-sailing on the billows.

We took a saw and several nails,
And water in the nursery pails;
And Tom said, “Let us also take
An apple and a slice of cake”;—
Which was enough for Tom and me
To go a-sailing on, till tea.

We sailed along for days and days,
And had the very best of plays;
But Tom fell out and hurt his knee,
So there was no one left but me.

===Chinese===
我们在楼梯上造了一条船，
全用里屋的椅子搭成，
还塞满了软软的沙发靠垫，
好去大浪上扬帆航行。

我们带上一把锯子和好几根钉子，
还有育儿房水桶里的水；
汤姆说：“咱们再带上
一个苹果、一块蛋糕吧”；——
这点东西对汤姆和我来说已足够，
够我们一直航行到吃茶点。

我们航行了一天又一天，
玩得真是再开心不过；
可汤姆摔了出去，磕疼了膝盖，
于是只剩下我一个人啦。

## Where Go the Boats? / 小船驶向何方？

===Original===
Dark brown is the river,
Golden is the sand.
It flows along forever,
With trees on either hand.

Green leaves a-floating,
Castles of the foam,
Boats of mine a-boating—
Where will all come home?

On goes the river,
And out past the mill,
Away down the valley,
Away down the hill.

Away down the river,
A hundred miles or more,
Other little children
Shall bring my boats ashore.

===Chinese===
河水是深棕色的，
沙子是金灿灿的。
它呀，流啊流啊不停歇，
两岸都是一排排的树。

绿叶子水上漂，
白泡沫像城堡，
我的小船也出航啦——
它们都会回到哪儿去呢？

河水不停地向前流，
从磨坊旁边流过去，
一直流向山谷底下，
一直流向小山脚下。

顺流而下，远远地，
一百多里地之外，
会有别的小孩子，
把我的小船带上岸来。

## Auntie’s Skirts / 姑姑的裙子

===Original===
Whenever Auntie moves around,
Her dresses make a curious sound;
They trail behind her up the floor,
And trundle after through the door.

===Chinese===
姑姑一走动起来，
她的衣裙就发出怪声响；
在地板上拖在她身后，
又滚呀滚地跟着穿过门框。

## The Land of Counterpane / 棉被王国

===Original===
When I was sick and lay a-bed,
I had two pillows at my head,
And all my toys beside me lay
To keep me happy all the day.

And sometimes for an hour or so
I watched my leaden soldiers go,
With different uniforms and drills,
Among the bed-clothes, through the hills;

And sometimes sent my ships in fleets
All up and down among the sheets;
Or brought my trees and houses out,
And planted cities all about.

I was the giant great and still
That sits upon the pillow-hill,
And sees before him, dale and plain,
The pleasant land of counterpane.

===Chinese===
我生病躺在床上的时候，
头底下垫着两个枕头，
所有的玩具都放在我身旁，
陪我开开心心过一整天。

有时候一两个钟头，
我看着我那铅做的小兵走，
穿着不同的军装、练着不同的操，
在被褥堆成的小山里头穿来穿去；

有时候我派出船队，
在床单上到处航行；
要么搬出我的树和房子，
到处建起一座座城。

我就是那个又大又安静的巨人，
端端正正坐在枕头山上，
眼前望着山谷和平原——
那片讨人喜欢的棉被王国。

## The Land of Nod / 瞌睡乡

===Original===
From breakfast on all through the day
At home among my friends I stay;
But every night I go abroad
Afar into the land of Nod.

All by myself I have to go,
With none to tell me what to do—
All alone beside the streams
And up the mountain-sides of dreams.

The strangest things are there for me,
Both things to eat and things to see,
And many frightening sights abroad
Till morning in the land of Nod.

Try as I like to find the way,
I never can get back by day,
Nor can remember plain and clear
The curious music that I hear.

===Chinese===
从吃早饭开始，一整天里，
我都待在家里，跟朋友们在一起；
可一到夜晚，我就出了门，
远远地走进那瞌睡乡。

我得一个人独自去，
没人告诉我该做什么——
孤零零地站在小溪旁，
爬上那梦境的山坡。

那里有最稀奇古怪的东西，
有吃的，也有看的，
还有许多吓人的景象出外头游荡，
直到瞌睡乡里天亮。

不管我怎么找路回去，
白天里我从来回不来，
也记不清、记不明白，
我听到的那古怪的音乐。

## My Shadow / 我的影子

===Original===
I have a little shadow that goes in and out with me,
And what can be the use of him is more than I can see.
He is very, very like me from the heels up to the head;
And I see him jump before me, when I jump into my bed.

The funniest thing about him is the way he likes to grow—
Not at all like proper children, which is always very slow;
For he sometimes shoots up taller like an india-rubber ball,
And he sometimes gets so little that there’s none of him at all.

He hasn’t got a notion of how children ought to play,
And can only make a fool of me in every sort of way.
He stays so close beside me, he’s a coward you can see;
I’d think shame to stick to nursie as that shadow sticks to me!

One morning, very early, before the sun was up,
I rose and found the shining dew on every buttercup;
But my lazy little shadow, like an arrant sleepy-head,
Had stayed at home behind me and was fast asleep in bed.

===Chinese===
我有一个小小的影子，跟我进进出出，
他到底有什么用处，我可真看不出来。
他从脚跟到脑袋，跟我像得不能再像；
我跳上床的时候，就看见他跳在我前面。

他身上最逗的一点，是他爱长大的样子——
一点儿也不像乖孩子，慢慢地、一点儿一点儿长；
他有时候会像皮球一样，“嗖”地一下蹿得老高，
有时候又会缩得那么小，小到连影儿都不见了。

他根本不懂孩子该怎么玩，
只会变着法儿地捉弄我。
他紧紧贴在我身边，一看就是个胆小鬼；
要是我像那影子黏着我似的去黏着保姆，我才觉得丢脸呢！

有一天大清早，太阳还没爬起来，
我就起了床，发现每朵毛茛上都亮着露珠；
可我那懒洋洋的小影子，活像个大瞌睡虫，
赖在家里没跟我来，还在床上睡得呼呼的。

## System / 规矩

===Original===
Every night my prayers I say,
And get my dinner every day;

And every day that I've been good,
I get an orange after food.

The child that is not clean and neat,
With lots of toys and things to eat,

He is a naughty child, I'm sure—
Or else his dear papa is poor.
===Chinese===
每天晚上我把祷告念，
天天都有三顿饭来填；

只要当天我乖乖听话，
饭后橘子就奖给我啦。

孩子若是不干又不净，
玩具零食数也数不清；

他准是个淘气小坏蛋，
不然就是他爹穷没钱。

## A Good Boy / 好孩子

===Original===
I woke before the morning, I was happy all the day,
I never said an ugly word, but smiled and stuck to play.

And now at last the sun is going down behind the wood,
And I am very happy, for I know that I've been good.

My bed is waiting cool and fresh, with linen smooth and fair,
And I must off to sleepsin-by, and not forget my prayer.

I know that, till tomorrow I shall see the sun arise,
No ugly dream shall fright my mind, no ugly sight my eyes,

But slumber hold me tightly till I waken in the dawn,
And hear the thrushes singing in the lilacs round the lawn.
===Chinese===
天还没亮我就醒来，一整天都高高兴兴，
我从不讲半句粗话，只是笑着玩个不停。

此刻太阳正慢慢落下，躲进那树林的背后，
我心里真是好快乐，因为知道自己乖得够。

我的小床正等着我，又凉又爽自自在在，
我得赶紧上床睡，可不能把祷告忘怀。

我知道，直到明天，我看见太阳再升起，
恶梦吓不坏我的心，丑象闯不进我眼底。

只等睡眠将我紧紧抱，直到我在黎明苏醒，
听见草坪四周丁香丛，鸫鸟唱得好听好听。

## Escape at Bedtime / 临睡前的逃奔

===Original===
The lights from the parlour and kitchen shone out
Through the blinds and the windows and bars;
And high overhead and all moving about,
There were thousands of millions of stars.

There ne'er were such thousands of leaves on a tree,
Nor of people in church or the Park,
As the crowds of the stars that looked down upon me,
And that glittered and winked in the dark.

The Dog, and the Plough, and the Hunter, and all,
And the star of the sailor, and Mars,
These shone in the sky, and the pail by the wall
Would be half full of water and stars.

They saw me at last, and they chased me with cries,
And they soon had me packed into bed;
But the glory kept shining and bright in my eyes,
And the stars going round in my head.
===Chinese===
客厅和厨房里的灯光透了出来，
穿过百叶窗、窗户和一道道铁栏；
高高的头顶上满天空摇摇摆摆，
千千万万颗星星闪亮在那半。

树上从没长出过那么多的叶片，
教堂或公园里也没这么多人丁；
像那成群结队俯身看我的星点，
在黑夜里眨着眼、亮亮又晶晶。

大犬、北斗、猎户，还有全部星官，
水手的那颗星，还有火星也在；
它们在天上闪耀，墙边水桶看看，
会盛上满满半桶水、半桶星海。

它们终于发现了我，叫嚷着来把我追赶，
没多会儿就把我打发上了小床；
可那辉煌的光芒还在我眼里闪亮好看，
满脑袋的星星团团转、好不忙。

## Marching Song / 行军歌

===Original===
Bring the comb and play upon it!
Marching, here we come!
Willie cocks his Highland bonnet,
Johnnie beats the drum.

Mary Jane commands the party,
Peter leads the rear;
Feet in time, alert and hearty,
Each a Grenadier!

All in the most martial manner
Marching double-quick;
While the napkin like a banner
Waves upon the stick!

Here's enough of fame and pillage,
Great commander Jane!
Now that we've been round the village,
Let's go home again.
===Chinese===
拿来梳子当琴弹一弹！
行军啦，我们来啦！
威利歪戴高地帽看一看，
约翰尼把鼓敲打。

玛丽·简来指挥这一队，
彼得殿后压阵脚；
步子合拍，精神抖擞不畏，
个个都是掷弹兵好！

全用最最神气的军姿，
飞快地向前行军；
餐巾像旗帜一面挂起，
在棍子上面飞舞！

名气和战利品都已足够，
伟大的指挥官简！
既然我们已绕村子走够，
我们这就回家转。

## The Cow / 奶牛

===Original===
The friendly cow, all red and white,
I love with all my heart:
She gives me cream with all her might,
To eat with apple-tart.

She wanders lowing here and there,
And yet she cannot stray,
All in the pleasant open air,
The pleasant light of day;

And blown by all the winds that pass,
And wet with all the showers,
She walks among the meadow grass
And eats the meadow flowers.
===Chinese===
那友善的奶牛，红白相间，
我打心眼里爱她：
她拼尽全力给我奶油鲜，
配苹果馅饼吃下。

她这儿那儿游荡，哞哞叫唤，
可她却走不了多远，
都在这宜人的开阔天，
这宜人的日光灿烂；

被所有路过的风儿吹拂，
被所有的阵雨淋湿，
她走在草地青青的小路，
吃着草地上的花枝。

## Happy Thought / 快乐的想法

===Original===
The world is so full of a number of things,
I'm sure we should all be as happy as kings.
===Chinese===
这世界装满了各种各样好东西，
我敢说我们都该像国王般欢喜。

## The Wind / 风

===Original===
I saw you toss the kites on high
And blow the birds about the sky;
And all around I heard you pass,
Like ladies' skirts across the grass—
O wind, a-blowing all day long,
O wind, that sings so loud a song!

I saw the different things you did,
But always you yourself you hid.
I felt you push, I heard you call,
I could not see yourself at all—
O wind, a-blowing all day long,
O wind, that sings so loud a song!

O you that are so strong and cold,
O blower, are you young or old?
Are you a beast of field and tree,
Or just a stronger child than me?
O wind, a-blowing all day long,
O wind, that sings so loud a song!
===Chinese===
我看见你把风筝抛上天，
又把鸟儿吹得满天转；
我四处听见你走过身边，
像淑女的裙摆掠过草面——
风啊，整整一天吹个不住，
风啊，唱着这么响亮的歌谱！

我看见你做的种种事情，
可你总是把自己藏隐。
我感到你推，我听见你唤，
我却怎么也看不见你的脸——
风啊，整整一天吹个不住，
风啊，唱着这么响亮的歌谱！

你这般强劲又这般冰凉，
风啊，你年老还是年幼长？
你是田野林木里的一只兽，
还是个比我更强的小孩头？
风啊，整整一天吹个不住，
风啊，唱着这么响亮的歌谱！

## Keepsake Mill / 纪念磨坊

===Original===
Over the borders, a sin without pardon,
Breaking the branches and crawling below,
Out through the breach in the wall of the garden,
Down by the banks of the river, we go.

Here is the mill with the humming of thunder,
Here is the weir with the wonder of foam,
Here is the sluice with the race running under—
Marvellous places, though handy to home!

Sounds of the village grow stiller and stiller,
Stiller the note of the birds on the hill;
Dusty and dim are the eyes of the miller,
Deaf are his ears with the moil of the mill.

Years may go by, and the wheel in the river,
Wheel as it wheels for us, children, today,
Wheel and keep roaring and foaming forever,
Long after all of the boys are away.

Home from the Indies, and home from the ocean,
Heroes and soldiers we all shall come home;
Still we shall find the old mill-wheel in motion,
Turning and churning that river to foam.

You with the bean that I gave when we quarrelled,
I with your marble of Saturday last,
Honoured and old and all gaily apparelled,
Here we shall meet and remember the past.
===Chinese===
越过了边界，是饶恕不了的罪过，
折断了树枝，从底下匍匐穿行，
钻出花园墙上的那一个缺口，
顺着河边堤岸，我们一路前进。

这儿是磨坊，轰鸣如阵阵雷响，
这儿是堰坝，泛起奇妙的白沫，
这儿是水闸，水流在底下奔淌——
离家虽近，却是神奇的好场所！

村子里的声响越来越静悄悄，
山上鸟儿的啼鸣也越来越轻；
磨坊主的眼睛又灰暗又粗糙，
耳朵被磨坊的劳作震得听不清。

一年年过去，河里那只水轮，
仍像今天为我们孩子般转动，
永远不停地轰鸣、翻着水花飞奔，
在所有男孩都已离去之后许久。

从东印度群岛归来，从大洋归来，
英雄和士兵，我们都要回故乡；
我们仍会看见那老水轮转得欢，
翻着搅着，把河水搅成白沫浪。

你带着我吵架时给的那颗豆，
我带着你上星期六的那颗弹珠，
庄重又老旧，一身盛装锦绣，
我们要在这儿相见，把往事故重温述。

## Good and Bad Children / 好孩子和坏孩子

===Original===
Children, you are very little,
And your bones are very brittle;
If you would grow great and stately,
You must try to walk sedately.

You must still be bright and quiet,
And content with simple diet;
And remain, through all bewild'ring,
Innocent and honest children.

Happy hearts and happy faces,
Happy play in grassy places—
That was how, in ancient ages,
Children grew to kings and sages.

But the unkind and the unruly,
And the sort who eat unduly,
They must never hope for glory—
Theirs is quite a different story!

Cruel children, crying babies,
All grow up as geese and gabies,
Hated, as their age increases,
By their nephews and their nieces.
===Chinese===
孩子们啊，你们还小小的，
骨头也都还脆又易折的；
你们想要长得高大体面，
走路就得稳稳当当、不乱颠。

你们还要活泼又安静，
粗茶淡饭也要心满意；
还要经过一切迷乱境，
做个天真诚实的好孩子。

快乐的心、快乐的脸，
草地上快乐地游玩——
古时候就是这么样的，
孩子们才长成了君王圣贤。

可那凶巴巴、不听话的，
还有贪吃没有节制的，
他们别想有什么光荣命——
他们的故事可是大不同！

残忍的孩子、爱哭的娃娃，
长大都成了蠢鹅和傻瓜，
年纪越大越招人厌弃，
被侄儿侄女们所嫌弃。

## Foreign Children / 外国的小孩

===Original===
Little Indian, Sioux or Crow,
Little frosty Eskimo,
Little Turk or Japanee,
O! don't you wish that you were me?

You have seen the scarlet trees
And the lions over seas;
You have eaten ostrich eggs,
And turned the turtles off their legs.

Such a life is very fine,
But it's not so nice as mine;
You must often, as you trod,
Have wearied not to be abroad.

You have curious things to eat,
I am fed on proper meat;
You must dwell beyond the foam,
But I am safe and live at home.

Little Indian, Sioux or Crow,
Little frosty Eskimo,
Little Turk or Japanee,
O! don't you wish that you were me?
===Chinese===
小小的印第安人，苏族或克劳族，
小小的冻坏了的爱斯基摩人，
小小的土耳其人或日本人，
哦！你不想变成我这样的人？

你见过那火红的大树，
还有海那边的狮子出没；
你吃过鸵鸟的蛋，
还把海龟翻得四脚朝天。

这样的日子倒是挺好，
可不如我的日子妙；
你一路走一路想，
一定厌倦了不在家乡。

你有稀奇古怪的东西可吃，
我吃的是正经体面的肉食；
你得住在大洋的浪花外，
我可安安稳稳住在家里头。

小小的印第安人，苏族或克劳族，
小小的冻坏了的爱斯基摩人，
小小的土耳其人或日本人，
哦！你不想变成我这样的人？

## The Sun's Travels / 太阳的旅行

===Original===
The sun is not a-bed when I
At night upon my pillow lie;
Still round the earth his way he takes,
And morning after morning makes.

While here at home, in shining day,
We round the sunny garden play,
Each little Indian sleepy-head
Is being kissed and put to bed.

And when at eve I rise from tea,
Day dawns beyond the Atlantic Sea,
And all the children in the West
Are getting up and being dressed.
===Chinese===
太阳并没有上床睡觉，
当夜里我枕着枕头躺倒；
他仍绕着地球一路走，
把一个又一个早晨造就。

当我们在家，在这亮堂的天，
绕着阳光满满的花园游玩，
每一个印第安困困小脑袋，
正被亲一亲、抱上了床铺睡。

而当傍晚我喝完茶起身，
大西洋那边已拂晓清晨，
西方所有的孩子们一个个，
正在起床、把衣裳穿妥。

## The Lamplighter / 点灯人

===Original===
My tea is nearly ready and the sun has left the sky;
It's time to take the window to see Leerie going by;
For every night at tea-time and before you take your seat,
With lantern and with ladder he comes posting up the street.

Now Tom would be a driver and Maria go to sea,
And my papa's a banker and as rich as he can be;
But I, when I am stronger, and can choose what I'm to do,
O Leerie, I'll go round at night and light the lamps with you!

For we are very lucky, with a lamp before the door,
And Leerie stops to light it as he lights so many more;
And O! before you hurry by with ladder and with light,
O Leerie, see a little child and nod to him tonight!
===Chinese===
我的茶点快好了，太阳也已下了天；
是时候趴到窗前，看利里路过这边；
因为每天晚上喝茶时，你还没坐上座，
他就提着灯、扛着梯，匆匆从街上走过。

如今汤姆想赶车，玛丽亚想下海洋，
我爸爸是个银行家，富得不能再富足；
可我呀，等我长大了，能自己选做的事，
哦利里，我夜里要跟你去，把一盏盏灯点起！

因为我们真有福气，门前有一盏灯立，
利里停下来把它点亮，像点亮许多盏一样；
哦！在你扛着梯、提着灯匆匆走过之前，
哦利里，看看这小孩，今晚对他点点头眼！

## My Bed Is a Boat / 我的床是条小船

===Original===
My bed is like a little boat;
Nurse helps me in when I embark;
She girds me in my sailor's coat
And starts me in the dark.

At night, I go on board and say
Good night to all my friends on shore;
I shut my eyes and sail away
And see and hear no more.

And sometimes things to bed I take,
As prudent sailors have to do:
Perhaps a slice of wedding-cake,
Perhaps a toy or two.

All night across the dark we steer:
But when the day returns at last,
Safe in my room, beside the pier,
I find my vessel fast.
===Chinese===
我的床像一条小小的船；
保姆扶我上去，当我登船；
她给我系上水手的外套，
就让我出发进了黑天。

到了夜里，我上了船，说道：
向岸上所有朋友道晚安；
我闭上眼睛驶向远方，
再也看不见、听不见。

有时候我把东西带上床，
像精明的水手必须做的：
也许一片结婚的喜糕，
也许一两个玩具小宝贝。

整夜我们在黑暗中掌舵：
可当白天终于又转回头，
我安安稳稳在房间里，码头边，
发现我的船儿系得牢牢不丢。

## The Moon / 月亮

===Original===
The moon has a face like the clock in the hall;
She shines on thieves on the garden wall,
On streets and fields and harbour quays,
And birdies asleep in the forks of the trees.

The squalling cat and the squeaking mouse,
The howling dog by the door of the house,
The bat that lies in bed at noon,
All love to be out by the light of the moon.

But all of the things that belong to the day
Cuddle to sleep to be out of her way;
And flowers and children close their eyes
Till up in the morning the sun shall arise.
===Chinese===
月亮有一张脸，像门厅里的钟一样；
她照着花园墙上的小偷、过往，
照着街道、田野和港口的码头，
还有树杈里睡着的小鸟儿头。

那尖叫的猫、吱吱叫的老鼠，
房门前嚎叫的那条狗子，
正午还躺在床上睡觉的蝙蝠，
都爱在月光底下出门走动。

可所有属于白昼的那些生灵，
都蜷缩着睡去，躲开她的行踪；
花儿和孩子们都闭上了眼睛，
直到早晨太阳再升上天空。

## The Swing / 秋千

===Original===
How do you like to go up in a swing,
Up in the air so blue?
Oh, I do think it the pleasantest thing
Ever a child can do!

Up in the air and over the wall,
Till I can see so wide,
Rivers and trees and cattle and all
Over the countryside—

Till I look down on the garden green,
Down on the roof so brown—
Up in the air I go flying again,
Up in the air and down!
===Chinese===
你喜不喜欢荡上秋千去，
高高飞进那蓝天里？
哦，我真是觉得这最最有趣，
是孩子能做的最美事体！

高高飞起，飞过了院墙，
直到我能看得那么宽广，
河流、树木、牛群，样样都在
那广阔田野的一大方——

直到我俯看那青青的花园，
俯看那褐色的屋檐——
我又飞上了高高的天，
高高飞起，又落下来！

## Time to Rise / 起床啦

===Original===
A birdie with a yellow bill
Hopped upon the window sill,
Cocked his shining eye and said:
"Ain't you 'shamed, you sleepy-head?"
===Chinese===
一只小鸟黄嘴巴，
蹦到窗台上去啦，
顶着一颗亮眼睛说道：
"你不害臊吗，小懒猫？"

## Looking-Glass River / 镜面河

===Original===
Smooth it slides upon its travel,
Here a wimple, there a gleam—
O the clean gravel!
O the smooth stream!

Sailing blossoms, silver fishes,
Paven pools as clear as air—
How a child wishes
To live down there!

We can see our coloured faces
Floating on the shaken pool
Down in cool places,
Dim and very cool;

Till a wind or water wrinkle,
Dipping marten, plumping trout,
Spreads in a twinkle
And blots all out.

See the rings pursue each other;
All below grows black as night,
Just as if mother
Had blown out the light!

Patience, children, just a minute—
See the spreading circles die;
The stream and all in it
Will clear by-and-by.
===Chinese===
河水悠悠向前流淌，
这儿泛起微波，那儿闪一道光——
啊，多么干净的卵石！
啊，多么平顺的河面！

飘浮的花瓣，银色的小鱼，
铺底的水潭清澈得像空气——
小孩子多么向往
能住到那底下去！

我们看得见自己彩色的脸庞
漂浮在荡漾的水潭上
在那凉凉的深处，
朦胧而又清凉；

直到一阵风或一道水纹，
潜水的燕子、扑跃的鳟鱼，
一眨眼把它揉皱
又把一切都抹掉。

看那一圈圈水环彼此追逐；
水底的一切变黑得像夜晚，
就仿佛是妈妈
把灯吹灭了一般！

孩子们，再耐心等一会儿——
看那一圈圈波纹正慢慢消散；
这条河，还有河里的一切，
不一会儿又会变清。

## Fairy Bread / 仙童面包

===Original===
Come up here, O dusty feet!
Here is fairy bread to eat.
Here in my retiring room,
Children, you may dine
On the golden smell of broom
And the shade of pine;
And when you have eaten well,
Fairy stories hear and tell.
===Chinese===
快上这儿来呀，沾灰的小脚丫！
这里有仙童面包可以尝。
在我的这间静室里，
孩子们，你们可以开宴，
尝一尝金雀花金黄的香气，
还有松树的绿荫；
等你们吃了个饱，
就听听、讲讲仙童的故事。

## From a Railway Carriage / 火车车厢里

===Original===
Faster than fairies, faster than witches,
Bridges and houses, hedges and ditches;
And charging along like troops in a battle,
All through the meadows the horses and cattle:
All of the sights of the hill and the plain
Fly as thick as driving rain;
And ever again, in the wink of an eye,
Painted stations whistle by.

Here is a child who clambers and scrambles,
All by himself and gathering brambles;

Here is a tramp who stands and gazes;
And there is the green for stringing the daisies!

Here is a cart run away in the road
Lumping along with man and load;
And here is a mill, and there is a river:
Each a glimpse and gone forever!
===Chinese===
比仙子还快，比女巫还快，
桥梁和房屋，篱笆和水沟；
又像战场上的大军冲锋，
草地上到处是牛群马群：
山岗和平原上所有的景象
像暴雨一样密密地飞过；
一次又一次，一眨眼的工夫，
漆着彩色的车站呼啸而过。

这儿有个孩子爬上又爬下，
独自一个，在摘那野莓的刺藤；

这儿有个流浪汉站着发呆；
那边是一片草地，好串起雏菊花环！

这儿有辆大车在路上失控逃窜，
连人带货一路颠颠簸簸；
这边是一座磨坊，那边是一条河：
每一眼都只一瞥，便永远不见！

## Winter-Time / 冬日

===Original===
Late lies the wintry sun a-bed,
A frosty, fiery sleepy-head;
Blinks but an hour or two; and then,
A blood-red orange, sets again.

Before the stars have left the skies,
At morning in the dark I rise;
And shivering in my nakedness,
By the cold candle, bathe and dress.

Close by the jolly fire I sit
To warm my frozen bones a bit;
Or with a reindeer-sled explore
The colder countries round the door.

When, to go out, my nurse doth wrap
Me in my comforter and cap,
The cold wind burns my face, and blows
Its frosty pepper up my nose.

Black are my steps on silver sod;
Thick blows my frosty breath abroad;
And tree and house, and hill and lake,
Are frosted like a wedding-cake.
===Chinese===
冬天的太阳赖着不起床，
一个结着霜、红通通的瞌睡虫；
眨一两个钟头的眼；然后，
像一个血红的橘子，又落了下去。

星星还没离开天空，
清早天没亮我就起了床；
我光着身子，冷得打哆嗦，
就着冰冷的蜡烛，洗澡、穿衣。

我挨着欢快的炉火坐着，
把我冻僵的骨头暖一暖；
要不就驾着驯鹿雪橇去探险，
去探访门外更冷的地方。

等到要出门，保姆就给我
裹上围巾、戴好帽子，
冷风烧灼着我的脸，又把
那霜做的胡椒粉吹进我鼻子。

在银色的草地上，我的脚印黑黑；
我呼出的霜气一股股地往外冒；
那树和房子，那山和湖，
全结了霜，就像一只结婚蛋糕。

## The Hayloft / 干草阁

===Original===
Through all the pleasant meadow-side
The grass grew shoulder-high,
Till the shining scythes went far and wide
And cut it down to dry.

These green and sweetly smelling crops
They led in wagons home;
And they piled them here in mountain tops
For mountaineers to roam.

Here is Mount Clear, Mount Rusty-Nail,
Mount Eagle and Mount High;—
The mice that in these mountains dwell
No happier are than I!

O what a joy to clamber there,
O what a place for play,
With the sweet, the dim, the dusty air,
The happy hills of hay.
===Chinese===
在那整片宜人的草坡上，
草长得齐肩高，
直到亮闪闪的镰刀四处挥舞，
把它割倒晒干。

这些绿油油、香喷喷的庄稼，
被装在大车上拉回了家；
在这儿堆成了一座座山头，
好让登山家们来攀爬。

这儿是"清山"、"锈钉山"、
"鹰山"和"高山"；——
住在这群山里的小老鼠，
也不见得比我更快乐！

啊，在那儿攀爬多开心，
啊，那是个多好的玩耍地，
空气又香、又暗、又带着尘，
那些快活的干草堆成的山。

## Farewell to the Farm / 别了，农庄

===Original===
The coach is at the door at last;
The eager children, mounting fast
And kissing hands, in chorus sing:
Goodbye, goodbye, to everything!

To house and garden, field and lawn,
The meadow-gates we swang upon,
To pump and stable, tree and swing,
Goodbye, goodbye, to everything!

And fare you well forevermore,
O ladder at the hayloft door,
O hayloft where the cobwebs cling,
Goodbye, goodbye, to everything!

Crack goes the whip, and off we go;
The trees and houses smaller grow;
Last, round the woody turn we swing:
Goodbye, goodbye, to everything!
===Chinese===
马车终于停在了门口；
急切的孩子，飞快地爬上车，
挥着小手，齐声唱起来：
再见啦，再见啦，所有的一切！

再见了房子和花园、田野和草坪，
再见了我们荡来荡去的草场栅门，
再见了水泵和马厩、大树和秋千，
再见啦，再见啦，所有的一切！

永远地别了啊，
干草阁门前的那架梯子，
挂着蜘蛛网的干草阁，
再见啦，再见啦，所有的一切！

鞭子啪的一响，我们出发了；
树和房子变得越来越小；
最后，我们绕过那树木茂密的拐角：
再见啦，再见啦，所有的一切！

## North-West Passage / 西北航程

### I / 一

#### Good Night / 晚安

===Original===
When the bright lamp is carried in,
The sunless hours again begin;
O'er all without, in field and lane,
The haunted night returns again.

Now we behold the embers flee
About the firelit hearth; and see
Our faces painted as we pass,
Like pictures, on the window-glass.

Must we to bed indeed? Well then,
Let us arise and go like men,
And face with an undaunted tread
The long black passage up to bed.

Farewell, O brother, sister, sire!
O pleasant party round the fire!
The songs you sing, the tales you tell,
Till far tomorrow, fare ye well!
===Chinese===
明亮的灯端进屋里的时候，
没有太阳的时光又重新开始；
在外面，在田野和小径上，
鬼魅出没的黑夜又回来了。

此刻我们看见余烬四散逃窜，
在炉火照亮的壁炉边；又看见
我们走过时，自己的脸
像画像一样，映在窗玻璃上。

我们真非得上床不可吗？那么好吧，
让我们像男子汉一样站起身、走出去，
迈着无所畏惧的步子，
面对那条通往床边的又长又黑的走道。

别了，我的兄弟、姐妹、父亲！
围在炉火边的快乐伙伴！
你们唱的歌、你们讲的故事，
在遥远的明天到来之前，且好好道别吧！

### II / 二

#### Shadow March / 影子行军

===Original===
All round the house is the jet-black night;
It stares through the window-pane;
It crawls in the corners, hiding from the light,
And it moves with the moving flame.

Now my little heart goes a-beating like a drum,
With the breath of the Bogie in my hair;
And all round the candle the crooked shadows come
And go marching along up the stair.

The shadow of the balusters, the shadow of the lamp,
The shadow of the child that goes to bed—
All the wicked shadows coming, tramp, tramp, tramp,
With the black night overhead.
===Chinese===
屋子四周是漆黑的夜；
它隔着窗玻璃往里张望；
它在角落里爬行，躲着光，
又随着晃动的火苗而移动。

此刻我小小的心儿咚咚跳得像擂鼓，
觉得妖怪的鼻息就喷在我头发上；
蜡烛四周，那些弯弯曲曲的影子走来，
一路列队行军，上了楼梯。

栏杆的影子，灯的影子，
那个去上床的孩子的影子——
所有那些邪恶的影子走来了，嚓、嚓、嚓，
头顶上是黑沉沉的夜。

### III / 三

#### In Port / 入港

===Original===
Last, to the chamber where I lie
My fearful footsteps patter nigh,
And come from out the cold and gloom
Into my warm and cheerful room.

There, safe arrived, we turned about
To keep the coming shadows out,
And close the happy door at last
On all the perils that we passed.

Then, when mamma goes by to bed,
She shall come in with tip-toe tread,
And see me lying warm and fast
And in the land of Nod at last.
===Chinese===
最后，我那害怕的脚步踢踢踏踏，
走近我躺卧的卧房，
从那又冷又阴的地方，
走进我那温暖明亮的房间。

到了那儿，安全到达，我们转过身，
把追来的影子挡在门外，
终于把这扇快乐的房门关上，
把一路上经过的险情都关在外面。

然后，等妈妈路过去就寝，
她会踮着脚尖走进来，
看见我暖暖和和地躺得死死的，
终于到了瞌睡乡里头。

## The Unseen Playmate / 看不见的玩伴

===Original===
When children are playing alone on the green,
In comes the playmate that never was seen.
When children are happy and lonely and good,
The Friend of the Children comes out of the wood.

Nobody heard him and nobody saw,
His is a picture you never could draw,
But he's sure to be present, abroad or at home,
When children are happy and playing alone.

He lies in the laurels, he runs on the grass,
He sings when you tinkle the musical glass;
Whene'er you are happy and cannot tell why,
The Friend of the Children is sure to be by!

He loves to be little, he hates to be big,
'Tis he that inhabits the caves that you dig;
'Tis he when you play with your soldiers of tin
That sides with the Frenchmen and never can win.

'Tis he, when at night you go off to your bed,
Bids you go to your sleep and not trouble your head;
For wherever they're lying, in cupboard or shelf,
'Tis he will take care of your playthings himself!
===Chinese===
孩子们独自在草地上玩耍的时候，
那位从未露面的玩伴就走了进来。
当孩子们快乐、孤单又乖巧的时候，
孩子们的朋友就从树林里出来。

没有人听见过他，也没有人看见过他，
他的模样是一幅你永远画不出的画，
可他准会到场，无论是在户外还是在家，
只要孩子们快乐地独自玩耍。

他躺在月桂树丛里，他在草地上奔跑，
你敲响会唱歌的玻璃杯，他就放声高歌；
每当你快乐得说不出是为什么，
孩子们的朋友就一定在你身侧！

他喜欢自己小小的样子，讨厌长大，
你挖的那些洞穴，住进去的就是他；
当你玩起你的锡兵玩具，
总是站在法国人那边、永远赢不了的，也是他。

就是他，夜里你去上床的时候，
叫你快去睡，别再胡思乱想；
因为不管你的玩具躺在哪儿，碗橱里还是架子上，
都是他亲自替你照看你的宝贝！

## My Ship and I / 我的小船和我

===Original===
O it's I that am the captain of a tidy little ship,
Of a ship that goes a-sailing on the pond;
And my ship it keeps a-turning all around and all about;
But when I'm a little older, I shall find the secret out
How to send my vessel sailing on beyond.

For I mean to grow as little as the dolly at the helm,
And the dolly I intend to come alive;
And with him beside to help me, it's a-sailing I shall go,
It's a-sailing on the water, when the jolly breezes blow
And the vessel goes a divie-divie-dive.

O it's then you'll see me sailing through the rushes and the reeds,
And you'll hear the water singing at the prow;
For beside the dolly sailor, I'm to voyage and explore,
To land upon the island where no dolly was before,
And to fire the penny cannon in the bow.
===Chinese===
啊，我就是一艘漂亮小船的船长，
这艘船呀，在池塘上扬帆远航；
我的小船老是转来转去、兜着圈子；
可等我再长大一点，我就能弄清这个秘密——
怎么让我的小船一直驶向前方。

因为我打算变得像舵手娃娃那样小，
我还打算让那娃娃活过来；
有他在旁边帮我，我就可以扬帆起航，
在水面上扬帆远航，当快活的风儿吹起，
小船就一蹦一蹦地往下栽。

啊，到那时你就会看见我穿过灯心和芦苇，
你就会听见水在船头唱歌；
因为在那娃娃水手身旁，我要去远航、去探险，
登上一个娃娃从未到过的岛屿，
再从船头放响那一分钱的小炮。

## My Kingdom / 我的王国

===Original===
Down by a shining water well
I found a very little dell,
No higher than my head.
The heather and the gorse about
In summer bloom were coming out,
Some yellow and some red.

I called the little pool a sea;
The little hills were big to me;
For I am very small.
I made a boat, I made a town,
I searched the caverns up and down,
And named them one and all.

And all about was mine, I said,
The little sparrows overhead,
The little minnows too.
This was the world, and I was king;
For me the bees came by to sing,
For me the swallows flew.

I played there were no deeper seas,
Nor any wider plains than these,
Nor other kings than me.
At last I heard my mother call
Out from the house at even-fall,
To call me home to tea.

And I must rise and leave my dell,
And leave my dimpled water well,
And leave my heather blooms.
Alas! and as my home I neared,
How very big my nurse appeared,
How great and cool the rooms!
===Chinese===
在一口波光闪闪的水井旁，
我发现了一个很小很小的山谷，
还没有我的头那么高。
四周的石楠花和荆豆，
在夏天正开着花，
有些黄，有些红。

我把这小水洼叫做大海；
那些小山丘，在我眼里很大；
因为我实在很小。
我做了一只船，我建了一座城，
我把那些洞穴上上下下搜了个遍，
把它们一个不落地都起了名字。

我说，这一带都是我的，
头顶上的那些小麻雀，
还有那些小鱼苗。
这就是世界，而我是国王；
蜜蜂是特意为我飞来唱歌，
燕子也是为我飞翔。

我玩着，假装没有更深的海洋，
也没有比这更宽广的平原，
更没有别的国王，只有我一个。
终于，我听见妈妈在呼唤，
那是黄昏时分，从屋子里传出来，
叫我回家喝茶去。

我只好站起来，离开我的山谷，
离开我那带着小酒窝的水井，
离开我的石楠花。
哎呀！当我快走到家门口，
保姆看起来多么高大，
那些房间多么宽敞、多么凉快！

## Picture-Books in Winter / 冬日的图画书

===Original===
Summer fading, winter comes—
Frosty mornings, tingling thumbs,
Window robins, winter rooks,
And the picture story-books.

Water now is turned to stone
Nurse and I can walk upon;
Still we find the flowing brooks
In the picture story-books.

All the pretty things put by
Wait upon the children's eye,
Sheep and shepherds, trees and crooks,
In the picture story-books.

We may see how all things are,
Seas and cities, near and far,
And the flying fairies' looks,
In the picture story-books.

How am I to sing your praise,
Happy chimney-corner days,
Sitting safe in nursery nooks,
Reading picture story-books?
===Chinese===
夏天消退了，冬天来到——
结霜的早晨，冻麻的拇指，
窗台上的知更鸟，冬日里的秃鼻鸦，
还有那些图画故事书。

如今水都变成了石头，
保姆和我都能在上面走；
我们却仍能找到潺潺的小溪，
就在那些图画故事书里。

收起来的所有好玩意儿，
都在等候孩子们的眼睛，
绵羊和牧羊人、树木和牧杖，
就在那些图画故事书里。

我们可以看见万物是什么模样，
海洋和城市，无论远近，
还有那飞舞的仙子们的容颜，
就在那些图画故事书里。

我要怎样才能歌颂你们呢，
那些围着炉角的快乐时光，
安安稳稳坐在育婴室的角落里，
读着图画故事书？

## My Treasures / 我的宝贝

===Original===
These nuts, that I keep in the back of the nest
Where all my lead soldiers are lying at rest,
Were gathered in autumn by nursie and me
In a wood with a well by the side of the sea.

This whistle we made (and how clearly it sounds!)
By the side of a field at the end of the grounds.
Of a branch of a plane, with a knife of my own,
It was nursie who made it, and nursie alone!

The stone, with the white and the yellow and grey,
We discovered I cannot tell *how* far away;
And I carried it back, although weary and cold,
For, though father denies it, I'm sure it is gold.

But of all of my treasures the last is the king,
For there's very few children possess such a thing;
And that is a chisel, both handle and blade,
Which a man who was really a carpenter made.
===Chinese===
这些坚果，我藏在窝的里头，
我的铅兵们都在那儿好好地躺着，
是秋天里保姆和我一起捡来的，
就在海边一口井旁的树林里。

这只哨子，是我们做的（吹起来多响亮！）
就在场地尽头那片田边做的。
用的是一截梧桐树枝，用我自己的小刀，
可全是保姆一个人做的，就保姆一个！

这块石头，带着白、黄、灰三种颜色，
是在多远的地方发现的，我说*不上来*；
我把它背了回来，虽然又累又冷，
因为虽说爸爸不承认，我肯定它是金子。

但我所有的宝贝里，最后这一件是国王，
因为很少有孩子能拥有这样的东西；
那是一把凿子，把手和刀刃俱全，
是一个真正的木匠亲手做的。

## Block City / 积木城

===Original===
What are you able to build with your blocks?
Castles and palaces, temples and docks.
Rain may keep raining, and others go roam,
But I can be happy and building at home.

Let the sofa be mountains, the carpet be sea,
There I'll establish a city for me:
A kirk and a mill and a palace beside,
And a harbour as well where my vessels may ride.

Great is the palace with pillar and wall,
A sort of a tower on the top of it all,
And steps coming down in an orderly way
To where my toy vessels lie safe in the bay.

This one is sailing and that one is moored:
Hark to the song of the sailors on board!
And see, on the steps of my palace, the kings
Coming and going with presents and things!

Now I have done with it, down let it go!
All in a moment the town is laid low.
Block upon block lying scattered and free,
What is there left of my town by the sea?

Yet, as I saw it, I see it again,
The kirk and the palace, the ships and the men,
And as long as I live, and where'er I may be,
I'll always remember my town by the sea.
===Chinese===
用你的积木能搭起什么？
城堡和宫殿、庙宇和码头。
雨尽管下吧，别人尽管去游荡，
我可以在家里开开心心地盖房子。

让沙发当高山，让地毯当大海，
我要在那儿给自己建一座城：
一座教堂、一座磨坊，外加一座宫殿，
还有一个港口，让我的船只停泊。

宫殿多么宏伟，有柱子也有墙，
顶上还带着一座塔楼模样，
台阶一级一级整整齐齐地下来，
一直下到我的玩具船安稳停泊的海湾。

这一艘正扬帆，那一艘正下锚：
听哪，船上水手们唱起了歌！
看哪，在我宫殿的台阶上，国王们
带着礼物和各样的东西来来往往！

如今我玩够了，让它倒下去吧！
一眨眼，整座城就平了。
一块积木叠一块积木，散落得到处都是，
我那座海边的小城，还剩下什么？

可是，我曾经见过它的模样，如今仍看得见，
那教堂和宫殿，那船只和人群，
而且只要我活着，无论身在何方，
我都会永远记得我那海边的小城。

## The Land of Story-Books / 故事书的国度

===Original===
At evening when the lamp is lit,
Around the fire my parents sit;
They sit at home and talk and sing,
And do not play at anything.

Now, with my little gun, I crawl
All in the dark along the wall,
And follow round the forest track
Away behind the sofa back.

There, in the night, where none can spy,
All in my hunter's camp I lie,
And play at books that I have read
Till it is time to go to bed.

These are the hills, these are the woods,
These are my starry solitudes;
And there the river by whose brink
The roaring lions come to drink.

I see the others far away
As if in firelit camp they lay,
And I, like to an Indian scout,
Around their party prowled about

So, when my nurse comes in for me,
Home I return across the sea,
And go to bed with backward looks
At my dear land of Story-books.
===Chinese===
傍晚时分，灯点亮了，
爸爸妈妈围坐在炉火旁；
他们坐在家里又说又唱，
什么游戏也不玩。

此刻，我端着我的小枪，匍匐前进，
摸着黑，沿着墙根爬，
顺着那条林间小道走啊走，
一直走到沙发靠背的那一头。

在那儿，在黑夜里，谁也看不见，
我躺在我的猎人营地里，
照着我读过的书里的故事玩耍，
一直玩到该上床的时候。

这些就是群山，这些就是树林，
这些就是我繁星点点的幽境；
那边还有一条河，河边
吼叫的狮子会来喝水。

我远远地看见别的人，
仿佛他们躺在火光闪闪的营地里，
而我，像个印第安斥候，
在他们的营地四周悄悄地转悠

所以，等保姆进来找我，
我就渡过大海回到家，
带着恋恋不舍的回望上了床，
望着我那亲爱的故事书的国度。

## Armies in the Fire / 火中的军队

===Original===
The lamps now glitter down the street;
Faintly sound the falling feet;
And the blue even slowly falls
About the garden trees and walls.

Now in the falling of the gloom
The red fire paints the empty room:
And warmly on the roof it looks,
And flickers on the backs of books.

Armies march by tower and spire
Of cities blazing, in the fire;—
Till as I gaze with staring eyes,
The armies fade, the lustre dies.

Then once again the glow returns;
Again the phantom city burns;
And down the red-hot valley, lo!
The phantom armies marching go!

Blinking embers, tell me true
Where are those armies marching to,
And what the burning city is
That crumbles in your furnaces!
===Chinese===
街灯此刻在街边闪烁；
足音落地，隐隐约约；
蓝色的暮霭缓缓降临，
笼罩着园中的树与墙垣。

如今暮色沉沉落下，
红红的火光把空房涂画：
它暖烘烘地映在屋顶，
又在书脊上明灭不停。

军队行经塔尖与钟楼，
那是一座座火海中的城郭；——
直到我瞪眼凝睇，
军队消散，光芒也熄。

而后那辉光又重回首；
幻影之城再度燃烧如旧；
看哪！沿那通红的山谷，
幻影般的军队正开步！

明灭的余烬，请如实相告，
那支军队要开往何方，
那座在你炉中崩塌的，
又是哪一座燃烧的城邦！

## The Little Land / 小小国

===Original===
When at home alone I sit
And am very tired of it,
I have just to shut my eyes
To go sailing through the skies—
To go sailing far away
To the pleasant Land of Play;
To the fairy land afar
Where the Little People are;
Where the clover-tops are trees,
And the rain-pools are the seas,
And the leaves like little ships
Sail about on tiny trips;
And above the daisy tree
Through the grasses,
High o’erhead the Bumble Bee
Hums and passes.

In that forest to and fro
I can wander, I can go;
See the spider and the fly,
And the ants go marching by
Carrying parcels with their feet
Down the green and grassy street.
I can in the sorrel sit
Where the ladybird alit.
I can climb the jointed grass;
And on high
See the greater swallows pass
In the sky,
And the round sun rolling by
Heeding no such things as I.

Through that forest I can pass
Till, as in a looking-glass,
Humming fly and daisy tree
And my tiny self I see
Painted very clear and neat
On the rain-pool at my feet.
Should a leaflet come to land
Drifting near to where I stand,
Straight I’ll board that tiny boat
Round the rain-pool sea to float.

Little thoughtful creatures sit
On the grassy coasts of it;
Little things with lovely eyes
See me sailing with surprise.
Some are clad in armour green—
(These have sure to battle been!)—
Some are pied with ev’ry hue,
Black and crimson, gold and blue;
Some have wings and swift are gone;—
But they all look kindly on.

When my eyes I once again
Open and see all things plain;
High bare walls, great bare floor;
Great big knobs on drawer and door;
Great big people perched on chairs,
Stitching tucks and mending tears,
Each a hill that I could climb,
And talking nonsense all the time—
O dear me,
That I could be
A sailor on the rain-pool sea,
A climber in the clover-tree,
And just come back, a sleepy-head,
Late at night to go to bed.
===Chinese===
当我独自在家中闲坐，
心中觉得好生厌烦，
我只需把双眼一阖，
便能航行过青天——
便能向远方航行，
去到那好玩的快乐国；
去到远远的仙女乡，
那是小精灵住的地方；
那里的三叶草顶是参天大树，
雨后的水洼是无边的大海，
片片树叶像一艘艘小船，
在小小的航程里扬帆；
而在雏菊树之上，
穿过草丛，
高高的大黄蜂
嗡嗡掠过空中。

在那片森林里来回闲逛，
我能漫游，我能徜徉；
看见蜘蛛和那苍蝇，
看见蚂蚁列队向前进，
它们用脚扛着一个个小包，
沿着那绿草如茵的街道。
我能在酸模丛中坐下，
就在瓢虫落脚的那一处。
我能攀上那节节草；
在高处
看硕大的燕子掠过
天空，
看圆圆的太阳滚着走过，
全然不把我这样的放在心上。

穿过那片森林我能前行，
直到——仿佛对着一面明镜——
嗡嗡的苍蝇、雏菊树，
连同我那小小的自己，
全都清清楚楚、明明白白，
映在我脚下那汪雨后水洼里。
要是有片小叶子飘落，
正好漂到我站立的一侧，
我便立刻登上那艘小船，
绕着雨洼的海洋来回转。

一个个爱思索的小生灵，
坐在它长满草的海岸上；
小东西们都生着可爱眼睛，
惊奇地看我扬帆起航。
有的披着一身翠绿的铠甲——
（这些准是打过仗啦！）——
有的五颜六色拼作一身，
黑的、绯的、金的、蓝的；
有的长着翅膀，转眼就远去；——
可它们全都和气地看着我。

待我把双眼再次睁开，
看清了眼前的一切本相：
光秃秃的高墙，光秃秃的大地；
抽屉和门上硕大的把手；
一个个大人高坐在椅上，
缝着褶裥、补着破洞，
每一个都是我能够攀登的山丘，
嘴上却整日说着些没要紧的话——
哎呀呀，
我要是还能
在那雨洼的海洋上当一名水手，
在三叶草树上当一个攀登家，
然后再回来，做一颗瞌睡脑袋，
到了夜里才上床歇下，该多好呀。

## Night and Day / 夜与昼

===Original===
When the golden day is done,
Through the closing portal,
Child and garden, flower and sun,
Vanish all things mortal.

As the blinding shadows fall,
As the rays diminish,
Under evening’s cloak, they all
Roll away and vanish.

Garden darkened, daisy shut,
Child in bed, they slumber—
Glow-worm in the highway rut,
Mice among the lumber.

In the darkness houses shine,
Parents move with candles;
Till on all the night divine
Turns the bedroom handles.

Till at last the day begins
In the east a-breaking,
In the hedges and the whins
Sleeping birds a-waking.

In the darkness shapes of things,
Houses, trees, and hedges,
Clearer grow; and sparrows’ wings
Beat on window ledges.

These shall wake the yawning maid;
She the door shall open—
Finding dew on garden glade
And the morning broken.

There my garden grows again
Green and rosy painted,
As at eve behind the pane
From my eyes it fainted.

Just as it was shut away,
Toy-like, in the even,
Here I see it glow with day
Under glowing heaven.

Every path and every plot,
Every bush of roses,
Every blue forget-me-not
Where the dew reposes,

“Up!” they cry, “the day is come
On the smiling valleys:
We have beat the morning drum;
Playmate, join your allies!”
===Chinese===
当那金色的白昼告终，
透过渐渐合拢的门扉，
孩童与花园、花朵与太阳，
凡间的一切全都隐退。

当那令人目眩的暗影垂下，
当那一束束光线渐弱，
在傍晚的斗篷底下，
它们全滚远，无影无踪了。

花园暗了，雏菊合拢，
孩童上了床，它们都入梦——
萤火虫亮在大车的辙痕，
老鼠在零乱的杂物中。

暗夜中一户户人家亮起灯，
父亲母亲举着烛火走动；
直到那神圣的夜色，
转动了一扇扇卧室的门。

直到最后白昼又开了端，
在东方，正一点点破晓，
在那一排排的篱笆与荆豆丛里，
酣睡的小鸟儿也醒来了。

暗影里的各样事物的形影，
房屋、树木、还有篱笆，
渐渐清晰起来；麻雀的翅膀
拍打着窗台与檐下。

这些声响会唤醒打哈欠的女佣；
她便去把那扇门儿打开——
只见园中草地上凝着露水，
而清晨也已经破开。

那边我的花园又重新生长，
涂染着碧绿与玫瑰色，
正如昨夜隔着窗玻璃，
从我眼前黯然隐没的那样。

正如它昨夜被关在窗外，
像玩具一般，在傍晚里，
这里我看见它在白昼中发亮，
在一片明亮的天穹之下。

每一条小径、每一方花圃，
每一丛盛开的玫瑰，
每一朵蓝蓝的勿忘草，
露珠在那上面安睡，

“起来呀！”它们嚷道，“白昼已到，
降临在那含笑的山谷：
我们已经敲响了晨鼓；
玩伴啊，快来和你的盟军会合！”

## Nest Eggs / 巢中的蛋

===Original===
Birds all the sunny day
Flutter and quarrel,
Here in the arbour-like
Tent of the laurel.

Here in the fork
The brown nest is seated;
Four little blue eggs
The mother keeps heated.

While we stand watching her,
Staring like gabies,
Safe in each egg are the
Bird’s little babies.

Soon the frail eggs they shall
Chip, and upspringing
Make all the April woods
Merry with singing.

Younger than we are,
O children, and frailer,
Soon in blue air they’ll be,
Singer and sailor.

We, so much older,
Taller and stronger,
We shall look down on the
Birdies no longer.

They shall go flying
With musical speeches
High overhead in the
Tops of the beeches.

In spite of our wisdom
And sensible talking,
We on our feet must go
Plodding and walking.
===Chinese===
鸟儿们整整一个晴日
扑着翅膀、叽叽喳喳地争吵，
就在这凉亭似的
月桂搭成的帐篷下。

就在那分叉的枝桠间，
安坐着一只褐色的鸟巢；
四枚蓝蓝的小小蛋儿，
由鸟妈妈暖暖地焐着。

当我们站着把她观望，
像傻瓜一样瞪大了眼，
每一枚蛋里却都平安地，
藏着鸟儿的小小娃娃。

不久那易碎的蛋儿便会，
啄破，而雏鸟一跃而起，
让整个四月的树林，
都因它们的歌声而欢喜。

它们比我们还要小，
孩子们啊，也还要娇弱，
